When these happen is open question Q4. If they stay at the end, they are task P7.9.

### Phase 2 — Core engine, transport, variables, vault, data model → v2.0.0

**Goal:** one correct request pipeline shared by all later features. The vault and history are trustworthy.

**Deliverable:** `packages/core`, a composer built on a `Draft`, fetch transport, IDB v5, the full variable system, vault v2, redaction, history v2, durability features, all body modes and response viewers.

**Delivery (D18):** four sessions. Each task is one PR from `main`; the order inside a session is the order below, because each task builds on the one before it.

| Session | Tasks | Stored data changes | PRs that wait for the maintainer |
|---|---|---|---|
| 2A — Foundations | P2.0, P2.1, P2.17, P2.3 | No | None |
| 2B — Data model v5 | P2.2, P2.12, P2.16, P2.10 | Yes | P2.2, P2.12, P2.16 |
| 2C — Variables, vault, history | P2.4, P2.6, P2.5 with P2.8 and P2.9, P2.11 | Yes | P2.6, the P2.5 PR, P2.11 |
| 2D — Viewers and release | P2.13, P2.14, P2.18, P2.19 | No | P2.13 (CSP), P2.19 |

**Rules for this phase (binding on every task):**
- `CLAUDE.md` applies in full. A bug found on the way gets a failing test first, an F-number in section 12 and an issue.
- No test is deleted, skipped or loosened to make a change pass. A test whose claim changes is changed together with the ledger row and the docs sentence, in the same PR.
- Each PR that closes a finding says `Closes #N`, removes the matching bullet from README "Known limitations", ticks its task here, and adds its deviations to section 16.
- Code moved out of `src/app` into `packages/core` moves its tests with it. The `src/app` coverage gate stays at 70% and the core gate at 90% from core's first PR.
- A task that touches a new browser API asserts 0 CSP and Trusted Types violation events in its e2e, in 3 engines (R15).
- Each session ends by recording in Appendix A: unit and e2e counts, both coverage numbers, initial bundle raw and gzip.

**Claims this phase changes** (each in the PR of the task named):

| Claim | Today | Becomes | Task |
|---|---|---|---|
| C-007 | A request that references a vault secret is blocked | A secret is sent resolved when the vault is unlocked, prompts for unlock when locked, and a placeholder never reaches the network | P2.5 |
| C-008 | History keeps the headers that were sent, including `Authorization`, in plain text | History stores what was sent with credentials and secret values masked | P2.5 |
| C-014 | Collection exports include auth fields in plain text | Exports mask credentials unless the user opts in for that export | P2.5 |
| C-003, C-004, C-005 | Per-secret key derivation; envelope v1 | DEK wrapped by a passphrase key; envelope v2; auto-lock | P2.6 |
| C-009 | Text renders as text; binary is a download | Adds HTML preview, image preview, hex view | P2.13 |
| C-017 | Seven methods | Any HTTP method, with the seven suggested | P2.16 |
| C-026 | Export and re-import is byte-identical | The same for format 2; format 1 files are refused (D24) | P2.2 |
| C-039 | Body tab only for POST, PUT and PATCH, with a Basic or JSON editor | Body modes none, raw, form, multipart, binary; no Body tab and no body for GET and HEAD | P2.12 |
| New | — | Cancel and timeout (P2.3); unresolved variables block the send (P2.5); passphrase rotation, vault export (P2.6); backup and restore (P2.11); the HTML preview's isolation (P2.13, C-048); what the browser changed (P2.14, C-049); nothing moves when a response arrives (P2.18, C-050) | as named |

  - encrypted vault export and import (the file holds the KDF parameters, the wrapped DEK and the envelopes; import asks for the file's passphrase and re-encrypts under the local DEK);

If it took more than 6, move P5.5 to P5.7 to section 15 and re-baseline section 9. **Recorded 2026-10-10: 4 sessions used (2A, 2B, 2C, 2D) of 4 planned. Nothing is moved: P5.5 to P5.7 (GraphQL, WebSocket, SSE) stay in Phase 5.** Ten sessions remain by the plan's count (Phases 3 to 7: 2, 3, 2, 2, 1).

### Phase 3 — Scripting on QuickJS with Postman compatibility (2 sessions) → v2.1.0

**Goal:** scripts work in production under strict CSP, cannot escape, and run the common Postman surface.

**Deliverable:** the QuickJS worker, the `pm`/`postman` API per matrix, the trust model, the compatibility doc, and the conformance suite.

**Delivery:** two sessions, 3A and 3B, released once as v2.1.0 (re-read against the repository and split in P3.0, v1.2.5). Each task is one PR from `main`; the order inside a session is the order of the table.

| Session | Tasks, in this order | Stored data changes | PRs that change security posture or a claim |
|---|---|---|---|
| 3A — The sandbox | P3.0, P3.1, P3.8, P3.2, P3.7, P3.6 | P3.8 adds `scriptTrust.hash` to a collection; nothing is removed and `DB_VERSION` stays 9 | P3.1 (CSP, C-006), P3.8 (new claim), P3.7 (new claim) |
| 3B — Postman compatibility and release | P3.5, P3.3, P3.4, P3.9, P3.12, P3.10, P3.11, P3.13 | No | P3.3 (`pm.sendRequest`), P3.12 (vault), P3.11 (C-040), P3.13 (release) |

Why this split, and why this order:

- **3A is whether a script may run and what it can reach; 3B is what it can call.** 3A adds no `pm` API: on QuickJS a script has the surface it has today (`pm.environment`, `pm.response`, `pm.test`, the small `pm.expect`, `console`), which is what C-040 says. So the sandbox is judged on its own, and the escape suite is written against a small surface before the large one arrives.
- **P3.8 comes second, not last.** P3.1 makes scripts run in every build. Since P2.2 an imported collection is marked untrusted, but nothing reads the mark. In 3A a script has no network, but it can set a variable, and the next request is sent to whatever address that variable now holds, with its secrets resolved. So the gate lands straight after the engine. `main` has one PR between them in which an untrusted script runs, as it did in every development build until now; nothing is deployed from it.
- **`pm.sendRequest` stays in P3.3 (3B)**, after the trust gate (D6) and the escape suite exist. Until then the escape suite's network assertion has no exception: 0 requests.
- **P3.7 before P3.6:** P3.6's AC puts its tests in the escape suite, which P3.7 creates. The limits themselves (memory, stack, the interrupt at the deadline) are set in P3.1; P3.6 gives them their messages, recycles the worker and tests them.
- **In 3B, P3.5 before P3.3:** `pm.expect` becomes chai inside the VM, and chai gets there through the build script P3.5 writes. P3.12 and P3.9 need `pm.request` and the variable scopes of P3.3. P3.10 measures the finished surface; P3.11 generates the document from the finished suite.

**Rules for this phase (binding on every task):**
- The rules of Phase 2 apply unchanged: `CLAUDE.md` in full; a finding gets a failing test, an F-number and an issue; no test is deleted, skipped or loosened; a test whose claim changes is changed with the ledger row and the docs sentence in the same PR; each PR ticks its task and adds its deviations to section 16.
- The sandbox is an allow-list. The VM's global object holds the ECMAScript built-ins and the names a task of this phase adds, and nothing else. A test lists the global's own property names and compares them with that list.
- Only JSON text crosses between the VM and the host, in both directions. No host object, function or handle is given to a script.
- The CSP gains `'wasm-unsafe-eval'` in `script-src` (P3.1) and nothing else. Never `'unsafe-eval'`, never `'unsafe-inline'` for scripts, no third-party origin; the Trusted Types policy keeps no `createHTML` and no `createScript`. A header that only a wider policy would satisfy is a stop: measure in three engines and ask (R15).
- Every e2e that runs a script asserts 0 CSP and Trusted Types violation events, in three engines.
- QuickJS and its `.wasm` file load lazily. Neither is in the initial chunk list. Budgets are the measurement after 2D (1,209,797 B raw, 306,352 B gzip) times 1.05 and 1.10, and are reset only by the PR that moves the baseline, with a changelog line.
- The service worker is not made to handle anything new: the `.wasm` file and the chunks are same-origin files with a content hash, which it already caches on first use. It still never touches a cross-origin request (C-015, F64).
- What a script writes (console lines, test names and messages, variable values) goes through `Redactor` with the request's secrets and credentials before it is shown or stored, and is added to the scan in `e2e/secrets-wire.spec.ts`.
- `pm.sendRequest` (P3.3) goes out through a `Transport`: `cache: "no-store"`, `credentials: "omit"`, `referrerPolicy: "no-referrer"`, `withDeadline`.
- A test does not assume a frame rate or a fast machine (R6): CI runners in 2D drew 2 animation frames in 500 ms and took 15 to 37 s for 2 s tests. A limit test asserts the message, not how long it took.
- Each session ends by recording in Appendix A: unit and e2e counts, both coverage numbers, the initial bundle, and the sizes of the `.wasm` file and the QuickJS chunk.

**Claims this phase changes** (each in the PR of the task named; new claims take the next free ID, and C-042 is left to PR #181):

| Claim | Today | Becomes | Task |
|---|---|---|---|
| C-006 | Scripts are disabled in the hosted app and say so with a banner | Scripts run in every build, in a QuickJS sandbox, under the production CSP | P3.1 |
| C-011 | The CSP forbids `eval` and inline script | Unchanged in words; its test also asserts that `'wasm-unsafe-eval'` is the only keyword beside `'self'` | P3.1 |
| New (C-051) | — | A script of a collection the user has not approved does not run | P3.8 |
| New (C-052) | — | A script reaches nothing but the API it is given: no network, no page, no storage; it is stopped at 5 s and 64 MB | P3.7, P3.6 |
| C-040 | A small `pm.*` subset | The surface in the compatibility matrix | P3.11 |
| C-046 | A restored collection's scripts are untrusted until approved | Unchanged; from P3.8 the words have an effect | — |
| New (C-053) | — | When a send's pre-request script moved the request to another host and the request uses a vault secret, the app asks first; on a no nothing is sent and what the script set is put back | P3.9 (Q6) |

**What the re-read changed (P3.0).** The tasks below are the plan's twelve, corrected where the repository had moved on:

- **F65 (#213), found in the re-read:** `RequestExecutor` reads `testResults` and `envMutations` from a script run and drops its `logs` and its `error`. A script that throws leaves no trace, and `console.log` goes nowhere, though `docs/scripts.md` says the Tests tab shows it. P3.1 shows the error; P3.2 shows the console.
- **P3.1 turns scripts on, so C-006 changes there, not in P3.11.** `SCRIPTS_ENABLED`, `SCRIPTS_DISABLED_ISSUE` and the banner are deleted with the old worker (the token's own comment says so), and P3.1's AC needs a script to run under the production CSP.
- **The engine is in core and takes the QuickJS module as an argument** (`packages/core/src/scripting/host.ts`), so that it runs in Node for core's tests and for the CLI (P6.5). The app's worker (`src/app/shared/scripts/quickjs.worker.ts`) loads the WebAssembly variant and hands it over. Core then needs `quickjs-emscripten-core` (MIT, 0.32.0), which is what the approved `quickjs-emscripten` re-exports; the umbrella package also depends on the debug and asyncify builds, which this plan rejected (section 5). P3.1 installs the core package and the one variant, and says so.
- **The VM's global grows by task.** P3.1 gives it `pm` (today's surface), `console`, `atob`, `btoa` and `setTimeout`. `require` arrives with P3.5 and `postman` and the legacy globals with P3.4.
- **`scriptTrust` holds `trusted` only.** The hash arrives with P3.8 (section 4.4, item 3). A collection has no scripts of its own until P4.9, so the hash covers the scripts of its requests.
- **`RoutePolicy` does not exist until P6.4.** `pm.sendRequest` is brokered through `TransportRouter`, which is what picks the route today.
- **No cookie jar until P5.3 and no runner until P5.2.** `pm.cookies` is in the matrix as unsupported until then; `pm.iterationData` is empty and `pm.execution.setNextRequest` is recorded and has no effect outside a run.
- **The redactor is at `packages/core/src/redact/redactor.ts`** (section 4.2 said `redaction/`).
- **The release had no task.** It is P3.13.
- **Two things for 3B to decide before it starts, not changed here:** P3.10 gates on wall-clock time on a CI runner, and 2D measured those runners at 7 to 18 times slower than a laptop from one job to the next, so a 300 ms p95 there may fail without a regression. And `newman` (P3.4) brings a large dependency tree: check `npm audit` before it enters the lockfile.

#### Session 3B — Postman compatibility and release

- [x] **P3.5** `require` shim for `crypto-js`, `lodash`, `uuid`, `chai`, `moment`, `atob`, `btoa`. Host-native shims (`@noble/hashes`) back crypto-js `SHA256`, `HmacSHA256`, `MD5`, `SHA1`, `HmacSHA1` and `enc.Base64/Hex/Utf8`. Any other module throws `WayfarerUnsupportedError: require('xml2js') is not supported — see docs/postman-compatibility.md#require`.

  - `pm.request` (`url` object with `toString`, `method`, `headers.add/upsert/remove/get`, `body` — mutable in pre-request and applied to the sent request);
  - `pm.response` (`code`, `status`, `headers`, `json()`, `text()`, `responseTime`, `responseSize`, `to.have.status/header/body/jsonBody`, `to.be.ok/success/error/clientError/serverError`);
  - `pm.test`;
  - `pm.expect` (full chai `expect` bundled into the VM);
  - `pm.info` (`eventName`, `iteration`, `iterationCount`, `requestName`, `requestId`);

  - `pm.execution.setNextRequest`/`skipRequest`: recorded, with an effect only in a run (P5.2);
  - `pm.sendRequest(req, cb)` and its promise form, brokered by the host through `TransportRouter` with the fetch options every user request has, max 10 per run, redacted in logs, and refused with the named error when the request it names holds a `{{$secret.` placeholder (C-007).
  - AC: the conformance suite `packages/core/test/pm-compat/*.test.ts` has one test per matrix row, and all pass in Node and in the browser worker; the escape suite's network assertion allows exactly the brokered calls.

**Exit criteria, Phase 3: met (2026-10-10), with one reading to note.**
- Scripts run in production in 3 browsers. *Met as "in the production build under the production headers": `e2e/scripts.spec.ts` and the F01 tripwire, in Chromium, Firefox and WebKit, with 0 CSP and Trusted Types events. The production site itself still serves a build from before Phase 1 (section 0); the deploy is the owner pass.*
- The escape suite and conformance suite are green. *Met: `e2e/sandbox-escape.spec.ts` (C-052) in three engines; the matrix in Node and in the worker (C-040).*
- The benchmark gate passes. *Met, as the gate was decided in P3.10: 64 to 88 ms on CI against a ceiling of 300 ms.*

### Phase 4 — Interop and auth (3 sessions) → v2.2.0

**Goal:** a user of Postman, Insomnia, OpenAPI or cURL can bring their work in and take it out, with an honest report of anything lost.

**Deliverable:** 5 importers, 4 exporters plus codegen, OAuth2, AWS SigV4, inheritance of auth, variables and scripts.

**Delivery:** three sessions, 4A, 4B and 4C, released once as v2.2.0 (re-read against the repository and split in P4.0, v1.2.7). Each task is one PR from `main`; the order inside a session is the order of the table. P4.6 is three PRs, (a), (b) and (c), because its parts are needed at three different points.

| Session | Tasks, in this order | Stored data changes | PRs that change security posture or a claim |
|---|---|---|---|
| 4A — What a collection holds, and the road in | P4.0, P4.9, P4.1, P4.6 (a), P4.5 | P4.9: a collection gains `auth` and `scripts`, a folder gains `variables`, `auth` and `scripts`, a request's auth may be `inherit`. `DB_VERSION` becomes 10 and empties `collections`, `folders`, `requests` and `files` (D24); the collection and workspace files become format 3 with no converter. The others: none | P4.9 (the trust gate covers inherited scripts; C-014, C-019, C-026, C-046, C-051, C-053), P4.1 (new claim), P4.6 (a) (C-020, new claim, the scan), P4.5 (new claim) |
| 4B — Postman and the other formats | P4.6 (b), P4.2, P4.4, P4.3 | P4.2: an optional `description` on a folder and on a request; no version change | P4.6 (b) (new claim, the scan), P4.2, P4.4, P4.3 (a new claim each; P4.4 brings `yaml`; P4.3 checks C-001 and may bring a parser) |
| 4C — Auth providers and release | P4.8, P4.7, P4.6 (c), P4.10 | P4.8: the `awsv4` auth type. P4.7: the `oauth2` auth type, and a token kept as a vault secret. Both add to what may be stored and change nothing that is; no version change | P4.8 (new claim, credentials in the redactor), P4.7 (a popup, a second HTML page, token storage; C-001; new claim), P4.6 (c) (C-035), P4.10 (release) |

Why this split, and why this order:

- **P4.9 comes first, not last.** Every importer maps onto what it adds: a Postman request's auth is "inherit" unless it says otherwise, a Postman folder and collection carry auth and scripts, an Insomnia folder carries variables. Importers written before it would flatten all of that and be rewritten after it. And an importer must not be able to write a collection-level script before the trust gate covers one: the same reason P3.8 came second in 3A.
- **P4.1 before any format.** It is the one road by which a file reaches the stores: read, map, validate, report, confirm, write. The Wayfarer formats that import today move onto it, so there is one import dialog and not two.
- **P4.6 (a) before P4.5:** P4.5's AC is `parse(buildCurl(req)) ≅ req`. Today's builder leaves out a multipart form and a file (see below), and lives in the app. The round trip needs a builder in core that writes every body mode.
- **P4.6 (b) before P4.2:** P4.2's AC exports back to Postman v2.1 and imports again.
- **In 4B, P4.4 before P4.3:** P4.4 is the smaller one and brings the YAML reader with its cap. P4.3 starts with a spike whose result goes to the maintainer; last in the session, a wait there holds nothing back.
- **The two auth providers are 4C.** No importer needs them to exist. An importer that meets OAuth 2.0 or AWS auth before 4C keeps none of it and says so in the report; P4.8 and P4.7 each add their type's mapping to the Postman, Insomnia and OpenAPI importers and to the Postman exporter, in their own PR. P4.7 is also where R8 and R15 are most likely to fire (a popup, a second page, three engines): a stop there holds no importer back, and v2.2.0 can ship with OAuth behind `settings.experimental.oauth2` if it must (section 10).
- **P4.6 (c) in 4C:** the HAR export is the only exporter nothing else waits for.

**Rules for this phase (binding on every task):**
- The rules of Phases 2 and 3 apply unchanged: `CLAUDE.md` in full; a finding gets a failing test, an F-number and an issue; no test is deleted, skipped or loosened; a test whose claim changes is changed with the ledger row and the docs sentence in the same PR; each PR ticks its task and adds its deviations to section 16.
- **An importer writes scripts unapproved** and never calls `approveScripts`. A collection it creates or replaces gets `{ trusted: false }`.
- **An imported file is untrusted, whatever its format.** An importer maps the file to the app's own model, and that result goes through the shared validators (`validateRequestContent`, `validateRows`) before anything is stored. The 10 MB cap and `readImportText` stay. An importer throws nothing but `ImportError` and ends in bounded time on any input (fast-check, section 10).
- **An import fetches nothing.** A file that refers to another file or to an address (an OpenAPI `$ref`, a Postman collection link) is not followed: the report says what was left out (C-001).
- A script is never handed a vault secret's plaintext by the app, and a script's variable changes go through `RequestExecutor.applyChanges`, which marks them `keepSecret`. Inherited scripts (P4.9) are scripts.
- **Whatever is stored, exported or copied goes through `Redactor`**, and a new place is added to the scan in `e2e/secrets-wire.spec.ts`. Every exporter and code generator of this phase is such a place, and so is a stored OAuth token.
- The matrix that P4.1's report scans against is `packages/core/test/pm-compat/cases.ts`; `docs/postman-compatibility.md` is generated from it (`npm run gen:compat`, `npm run check:compat`).
- No CSP or Trusted Types widening without a decision in section 6 (R15). A task that uses a browser API new to the app asserts 0 violation events in three engines. P4.7's popup follows D20: `BroadcastChannel`, not `window.opener`.
- **New code is written in `packages/core`** (`src/import/`, `src/export/`, `src/auth/`), where section 10 puts it and where the CLI (P6.5) needs it. A task moves there what it changes of `collection-io.ts`, `environment-io.ts`, `inspect/export.ts` and `request-auth.ts`, with its tests; it does not move what it does not touch.
- **Importers, the YAML reader, an OpenAPI parser and the code generators load lazily** (R9) and are absent from the initial chunk list, asserted as `e2e/scripts.spec.ts` asserts it for the engine. Budgets are those set in P3.13 (1286kb, 1347kb, 341,900 B gzip) and are reset only by the PR that moves the baseline, with a changelog line.
- A test asserts an outcome, not how long it took (R6). A time named in an AC is measured on a laptop and on CI and recorded in Appendix A; it becomes a CI gate only as a ceiling several times the slowest reading, as P3.10 decided.
- **Approved for this phase (maintainer, 2026-10-10):** the package `yaml`; Newman by `npx` at a pinned version with committed golden files, never in a `package.json`; six vendored fixture sets, each at a pinned commit with its LICENSE under `packages/core/test/fixtures/` and a line in `THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.md` (Newman's `test/integration`, a subset of Adyen's collections, Microsoft Graph's, the Petstore specifications, AWS's SigV4 test vectors, Insomnia export samples). Anything else is a stop. The OpenAPI parser is chosen by the maintainer after P4.3's spike.
- Each session ends by recording in Appendix A: unit and e2e counts, both coverage numbers, the initial bundle, the sizes of the lazy files it added, and the mutation score if `resolver.ts`, `redactor.ts` or `vault-crypto.ts` changed.

**Claims this phase changes** (each in the PR of the task named; new claims take the next free ID, C-054 onward, and C-042 is left to PR #181):

| Claim | Today | Becomes | Task |
|---|---|---|---|
| C-051 | The scripts of a collection that came from a file or a backup do not run until reviewed | Unchanged in words; "scripts" now includes the collection's own and its folders', and its test imports one of each | P4.9 |
| C-053 | The question before a secret goes to another host, for one send | Unchanged in words (maintainer, 2026-10-10: the limit stays as documented). Its comparison starts before the first inherited script and its test has one | P4.9 |
| C-019 | Bearer, Basic and API-key auth set in the Auth tab reach the server | Also when set on the collection or a folder and the request inherits | P4.9 |
| C-026 | Collection export (file format 2) and re-import is byte-identical | Restated for format 3 | P4.9 |
| C-014 | Collection exports mask credentials unless asked for | Also the credentials of the collection's and the folders' auth | P4.9 |
| C-046 | The workspace backup | Unchanged in words; the file is format 3 | P4.9 |
| New | — | An import shows what it will add and what it could not keep before anything is stored; cancelled, it stores nothing | P4.1 |
| C-020 | Copy as cURL copies a command for the current request | The command carries the body in every mode, and `HEAD` | P4.6 (a) |
| New | — | The request as code for `fetch`, Python `requests` and HTTPie, masked as the cURL command is | P4.6 (a) |
| New | — | A cURL command pasted into the address field or the import dialog becomes a request; a HAR 1.2 file imports | P4.5 |
| New | — | A collection and an environment export as Postman v2.1 files, and such a file imported again gives the same collection | P4.6 (b) |
| New | — | Postman v2.0 and v2.1 collections, environments and globals import, scripts unapproved | P4.2 |
| New | — | Insomnia v4 and v5 exports import | P4.4 |
| New | — | OpenAPI 3.0 and 3.1 and Swagger 2.0 import; a reference to another file or address is not fetched | P4.3 |
| C-001 | Apart from loading the app, the only network traffic is the request the user composes | Read against P4.3 (nothing is fetched for a `$ref`) and P4.7 (a token request and the popup go to the addresses the user typed). Reworded only if its sentence does not cover those, with its test | P4.3, P4.7 |
| New | — | AWS Signature V4 signs the request as sent | P4.8 |
| New | — | OAuth 2.0 with three grants; the app accepts only a `state` it issued; a token is kept in the vault, or in memory when the vault is locked | P4.7 |
| C-035 | Responses export as HAR 1.2; bodies over 256 KB or not JSON are left out | Reworded for what P4.6 (c) exports: text bodies, and several history entries in one file | P4.6 (c) |
| C-007 | A secret's plaintext is in nothing stored, exported or copied | Unchanged in words; the scan gains each exporter, the generated code and the stored token | P4.6 (a), (b), (c), P4.7 |
| README limitation for #78 | "No Postman/OpenAPI/cURL import" | Gone | P4.3 (the last importer) |

**What the re-read changed (P4.0).** The tasks below are the plan's nine, corrected where the repository had moved on or where the text could not be built as written. Each statement was checked on `main` at `e728bcb`.

- **P4.9's text had no scripts, and three other places say it has.** Section 4.4, section 12 (F30) and P3.8 as built all put collection- and folder-level scripts in P4.9; the task named auth and variables only. Postman's collection and folder events cannot be imported without them, and Newman's integration collections use them. P4.9 has them now, with the trust gate.
- **The model today is smaller than section 4.4's target.** `AuthConfig` is `none`, `bearer`, `basic`, `apikey`: no `inherit`. A `Collection` has `variables` and `scriptTrust`; a `Folder` has a name and a place. `validate.ts` lists the four auth types. A `Row` has no `description`, and neither has a folder or a request. `BODY_MODES` has no `graphql` (P5.5): until then an importer writes a GraphQL body as the JSON text it is sent as, and says so in the report.
- **Importers, exporters and auth are in the app, not in core.** `src/app/shared/collections/collection-io.ts`, `environments/environment-io.ts`, `inspect/export.ts`, `http/request-auth.ts`; the request is put together in `WorkspaceStore.assemble`. Section 4.3's `AuthProvider` does not exist; auth is three functions. See the rule above.
- **The cURL command leaves bodies out without a word.** `WorkspaceStore` hands the exporter `textOf(body)`, which is the body only when it is text: a multipart form and a file are dropped from the command, and from the HAR. The task knew `-F` was missing; it did not know nothing is written in its place. Part of F31; P4.6 (a).
- **`toHar` reads `location.origin`**, keeps a body only when it is JSON under 256 KB, and takes one entry. C-035 says so, and changes with P4.6 (c). The history list has no selection of several entries; that task builds it.
- **No runner until P5.2.** P4.2's "the Wayfarer runner" is the harness of `legacy-golden.test.ts`: the collection's requests one after another through `runScript`, `VariableResolver` and `FetchTransport`. A collection that needs `setNextRequest` is outside the matrix (the row is "partial: recorded"). P4.6's "HAR of a whole runner run" moves to P5.2.
- **Newman is not a dependency** (P3.4; maintainer again, 2026-10-10): `scripts/golden.mjs` is extended, the golden files are committed, CI holds Wayfarer to the files. The echo server has had a Postman Echo mode for these collections since P1.2 (`startEchoServer(port, host, { postmanEcho: true })`).
- **Fixture sizes, checked on GitHub on 2026-10-10:** Newman's `test/integration` has 42 entries (15 of them folders), Apache-2.0; `Adyen/adyen-postman` is MIT and 6.2 MB, so a subset is vendored; `microsoftgraph/microsoftgraph-postman-collections` is MIT, archived, 685 kB.
- **The matrix is test data.** `cases.ts` is under `packages/core/test/`, which app code does not import. P4.1 puts the rows' `api`, `status` and `note` where `src/` can read them and leaves the proving scripts in the test, or keeps a list that a test holds equal to the rows; the PR says which.
- **There is no YAML reader, and no format before P4.4 is YAML.** `maxAliasCount` is an option of the `yaml` package (2.9.1, ISC, no dependencies; approved). The cap and the alias-bomb fixture move from P4.1 to P4.4.
- **Two ACs name a time on a CI runner** (10 s for 5,000 requests, 1 s for the alias bomb). See the rule above; the 5,000-request file is made by the test and not committed.
- **`-k` cannot map to the bridge's insecure TLS:** the bridge has none until P6.1. Until then it is left out with a warning.
- **The 30 cURL fixtures are written, not captured** (maintainer, 2026-10-10): a session cannot press "Copy as cURL" in a browser's developer tools. They are written in the form each browser produces and labelled so; captured ones can be added.
- **"An identical `/echo` reflection" (P4.6) cannot be identical.** A browser and curl each add headers of their own (`User-Agent`, `Accept`, `Origin`, `Sec-Fetch-*`), and each writes its own multipart boundary. Compared: the method, the path and query, the headers the request sets, and the body (a multipart form part by part, a file byte for byte).
- **The mock identity provider exists** (`/oauth/authorize` and `/oauth/token` in `e2e/support/echo-server.mjs`: the code grant with PKCE, client credentials, password, refresh with rotation). It always answers `expires_in: 3600` and always sends CORS headers, so "the expired token refreshes" and "a token endpoint without CORS" need two additions to it, with their tests. **No e2e starts the Local Bridge** (three specs open its settings and no more); `docs/browser-limits.md` has no `#oauth` section, which R8 links to; `public/oauth/` does not exist.
- **F69, found in the re-read: the service worker would store the OAuth callback page as the app.** `networkFirstShell` in `src/sw.ts` puts every HTML answer to a navigation into the cache under the shell's key. Its comment says a navigation to a text file must not replace the shell; a second HTML page would. After one sign-in the offline app would be the callback page. No second HTML page exists today, so nothing is wrong on `main`; P4.7 fixes the rule before it adds the page, with a failing test first.
- **From a page with `Cross-Origin-Opener-Policy: same-origin`, the handle `window.open` gave reads as closed once the popup has gone to another origin.** The app cannot tell that from the user closing it. P4.7 needs a way to give up: a Cancel in the app and a time limit.
- **SigV4 signs what the browser sends, which the app does not fully write.** The browser sets `Host` and serialises the path its own way, and it writes a multipart body's boundary only when it sends. P4.8 signs the address as `URL` gives it and the body's bytes as they will go; for a multipart form it serialises first or refuses with a message, and says which.
- **An OpenAPI file's reference to another file or address is never fetched** (C-001). That removes most of what a parser library is for, so the spike has a third candidate: no library, with references inside the file followed by hand.
- **A send keeps one value per header name** (`WorkspaceStore.assemble`, the later row winning). Section 4.4 says "duplicates allowed", and that is true of what is stored. A file with two headers of one name imports both rows; the report says the second is the one sent.
- **Not in section 0 until now: #228**, opened in 3B. The WebKit e2e job on GitHub fails about one run in two on a claim test, a different one each time, and passes on the rerun. Every PR of this phase will meet it; a rerun that passes is recorded in `e2e/FLAKES.md` and in the PR.
- **The release had no task.** It is P4.10.
- **Findings and their issues:** F21 (#78) closes with the last importer, P4.3. F31 (#88) closes with P4.6 (a). F28 (#85) is half closed by P4.7; the cookie jar is P5.3.

#### Session 4A — What a collection holds, and the road in

- [x] **P4.0** Re-read Phase 4 against the repository and split it into sessions (section 9, Milestones). This text.
  - AC: the delivery table, the claims table, the rules and exit criteria per session exist; every statement about the code was checked on `main` at `e728bcb`; the maintainer's answers and a row are in section 16.
- [x] **P4.9** Inheritance of auth, variables and scripts: collection → folder → request. *(Done; section 16, v1.2.8.)*
  - Model (core): `AuthConfig` gains `{ type: "inherit" }`. A collection gains `auth` (never `inherit`) and `scripts: { pre, post }`; a folder gains `variables`, `auth` (`inherit` when new) and `scripts`. A request made in a collection starts with `inherit`; one in no collection has `none`, and `inherit` there sends no auth.
  - `effectiveAuth` (core, with `request-auth.ts` moved beside it): the request's own unless it inherits, then the nearest folder's that does not, then the collection's.
  - Variables: a folder's win over its collection's and lose to the environment's, as a collection's do today; the nearest folder first. `VARIABLE_SCOPES` in `resolver.ts` changes, so the mutation suite runs (85% or more).
  - Scripts: a send runs the collection's pre-request script, then each folder's from the outside in, then the request's; the post-response scripts in the same order. Each is a run of its own under the limits, with the variables the one before it left. An error in any pre-request script stops the send (P3.9). The matrix gains rows for what a script sees of the chain.
  - Trust: the digests of collection and folder scripts join `scriptTrust.approved`. `ScriptTrust.check` covers the request's scripts and its ancestors'; the review lists them. Saving a collection's or a folder's script here adds its digest under the rule a request's save has: only when the scripts were allowed to run before the save. `CLAUDE.md`'s sentence on who calls `approveScripts` is updated in this PR.
  - C-053: the comparison is of the request before the first inherited pre-request script and after the last. The limit stays as documented (maintainer, 2026-10-10).
  - UI: a settings dialog for a collection and for a folder (auth, variables, scripts), and in the Auth tab a line that says what an inheriting request will send and where it comes from.
  - Stored: `DB_VERSION` 10 empties `collections`, `folders`, `requests` and `files` and says so (D24; changelog and `docs/storage.md`). History is kept: no entry changes shape. The collection file is `wayfarer/collection/3` and the workspace file `wayfarer/workspace/3`; format 2 files are refused. `exportCollectionJson` and the backup mask the credentials of collection and folder auth too (C-014).
  - AC: unit tests for the inheritance chain (8 cases) in core; e2e: a collection's bearer auth reaches `/echo` for a request set to inherit, and a folder's replaces it; a collection script and a folder script written by an import do not run until reviewed, and run in order after; `idb-upgrade.spec.ts` and `e2e/upgrade.spec.ts` cover version 10; the mutation score is in the PR; screenshots of the dialog and the Auth tab's line read at 390 and 1440 px in both themes.
- [x] **P4.1** Import pipeline: *(Done; section 16, v1.2.9.)*
  - core: `ImportError`, `ImportReport` (counts; per-item warnings: unsupported auth type, body mode, script APIs found by static scan against the matrix, dropped fields), and the detection of a format from the file's content;
  - an importer is a function from text to collections and environments in the app's model, with ids from `newId()`, validated by the shared validators before it returns;
  - it runs in a worker (the page reads the file with `readImportText` and hands the text over);
  - a report UI shown before commit (Cancel/Import); one import dialog, which the Wayfarer collection and environments formats that import today move onto;

### Phase 5 — Workspace UX: tabs, runner, cookies, protocols (2 sessions) → v2.3.0

**Goal:** daily-driver ergonomics on par with desktop clients for REST, GraphQL, SSE and WebSocket.

**Deliverable:** multi-tab composer, in-app runner with reports, cookie manager, GraphQL, SSE and WebSocket.

**Re-read against the repository (P5.0, 2026-10-12, `main` at `ea8df75`), and the split.** The phase was planned at two sessions and is split into three, as Phase 4 was: P5.1 alone changes how the workspace's state is held and adds a store, and three tasks need the bridge to say more than it does.

| Session | Tasks | Why together |
|---|---|---|
| 5A | P5.0; F84, F85, F83 (found or decided before the first task); P5.1 | The findings are small and two of them are in what every later e2e loads. Tabs come first: the runner, SSE and WebSocket each show their result in a tab |
| 5B | P5.2, P5.6, P5.7 | No bridge and no new store. The runner is core code with a table; SSE and WebSocket are two streams with a list each |
| 5C | P5.4, P5.3, P5.5, P5.8 | The two bridge additions, then the cookie jar on top of them (`DB_VERSION` 12), GraphQL with its two dependencies, the release |

What the re-read changes in the tasks is written into each below, marked "P5.0".

**Tasks:**

- [x] **P5.0** Re-read of the phase against the repository, the maintainer's answers, the split. Section 16, v1.2.32.
- [ ] **P5.1** `WorkspaceStore`:
  - tabs persisted in a `tabs` store created by this task's IDB upgrade (draft plus last response reference); the strip is a `mat-tab-nav-bar` with one panel, per `CLAUDE.md`;
  - dirty indicator;
  - close-with-unsaved confirm;
  - restore on reload;
  - duplicate tab;
  - shortcuts per D9;
  - a tab bound to a saved request shows its breadcrumb.
  - AC: e2e: open 5 tabs, edit 2, reload, and all 5 restore with 2 dirty; closing a dirty tab prompts; the keyboard test passes in 3 browsers without triggering browser tab actions.
  - P5.0: (a) the `tabs` store is `DB_VERSION` 11; by D24 nothing is converted, and nothing needs to be, since no draft is stored today. (b) A stored draft holds what a saved request holds: a typed credential as text, a vault secret as its reference. The store joins the scan of `e2e/secrets-wire.spec.ts`, Reset all data, `docs/storage.md`, and the announcement to other tabs. (c) "Last response reference" is the key of the history entry of the tab's last send: a response is kept nowhere else, and history keeps it masked. A restored tab shows it as an entry opened from history is shown. (d) A file of a body that was never saved is in memory only (`RequestFiles`): a restored tab names it and asks for it again. (e) **To decide with the maintainer before the store is written:** what a restored tab's scripts are to `ScriptTrust` (D6 says a script that reached storage in any way but a review or a save here does not run), and whether two browser tabs share one set of tabs. (f) On macOS Option+T types "†": the shortcut is read from `event.code`. (g) `workspace-store.ts` is 949 lines; the per-tab state is a file of its own.
- [ ] **P5.2** Runner UI and `RunnerEngine` (core):
  - select a collection or folder;
  - iterations;
  - data file (CSV via RFC 4180 parser, JSON array);
  - delay;
  - stop on first failure;
  - `setNextRequest`/`skipRequest`;
  - live results table;
  - export JSON and JUnit XML, and a whole run as HAR 1.2 through the exporter of P4.6 (c) (moved here in P4.0: there is no run before this task);
  - Cancel;
  - C-053 is looked at again here (maintainer, 2026-10-10): its comparison is of one send, and a run is where a script of one request sets the host of the next. The task decides whether a run asks, and records it.
  - AC: a fixture collection with 3 requests × 5 data rows produces 15 results equal to a snapshot; JUnit validates against the JUnit XSD; Cancel stops within one request.
  - P5.0: (a) `RequestExecutor` (the app) is what sends with scripts, the vault and the trust gate; `RunnerEngine` (core) is the order of a run, the data file, `setNextRequest` and the three report formats, and is handed a function that sends one request. The CLI (P6.5) hands it another. (b) `ScriptResult.nextRequest` and `pm.iterationData` exist; the matrix rows "partial: recorded" become "supported" with their scripts, and `gen:compat` is run. (c) A run's scripts pass `ScriptTrust.check` like a send's. (d) **To ask:** which JUnit XSD is vendored and what validates against it.
- [ ] **P5.3** Cookie manager (a `cookies` store created by this task's IDB upgrade): shows jar entries (bridge-sourced `Set-Cookie`), edit and delete, applied to bridge-routed requests by domain, path and secure rules; the direct-mode notice explains the browser jar is not accessible.
  - AC: an e2e via the bridge: `/cookies/set` followed by `/echo` shows the `Cookie` header sent; deleting the cookie removes it from the next request.
  - P5.0: today's bridge joins repeated headers with a comma, and a joined `Set-Cookie` cannot be split again (a date holds a comma). The bridge's answer gains the header list as received (the first line of P6.1's protocol 2, moved here), which a page that does not know it ignores. `DB_VERSION` 12. A cookie's value is a credential to the redactor. `pm.cookies` stays unsupported unless this task says otherwise in the matrix. A HAR's `cookies` lists stop being empty for a bridge-routed exchange.
- [ ] **P5.4** Redirect inspection: in bridge mode, the full redirect chain (status, Location) is shown when "follow redirects" is on; when off, the 3xx response is shown as-is. Direct mode shows "Redirected → final URL" only, with an explanation.
  - AC: e2e `/redirect/3` via the bridge lists 3 hops.
  - P5.0: today's bridge follows nothing: a 3xx is what comes back. "Follow redirects" is `settings.followRedirects` of the request, which the model has and nothing reads. The bridge gains "follow, at most 20" and the chain in its answer (P6.1's line, moved here); it checks each hop's address as it checks the first. The direct half exists (`responseRedirectedTo`). Before P5.3, which must read `Set-Cookie` of every hop.
- [ ] **P5.5** GraphQL body mode: query editor with a variables pane; schema introspection (cached per URL, manual refresh) using `graphql`; autocomplete and validation in Monaco via `graphql-language-service` (lazy).
  - AC: e2e against the echo-server's GraphQL endpoint: introspection populates the schema explorer; an invalid field shows a validation marker; the query result renders.
  - P5.0: the echo server has no GraphQL endpoint; the task writes one, without a dependency of the server's. `graphql` and `graphql-language-service` are two new dependencies of the app: asked for before they enter the lockfile, with `npm audit`, their licences and the size of the lazy chunk. A Monaco language needs its register import, a worker wrapper and a stub mapping (`CLAUDE.md`, Monaco).
- [ ] **P5.6** SSE: streamed via `fetch` reading `text/event-stream` (so custom headers work), with an event list (id, event, data, time), stop/reconnect, and `Last-Event-ID` support.
  - AC: e2e `/sse` emits 5 events, and all 5 are listed with correct fields in 3 browsers.
  - P5.0: a `Transport` answers with a whole body; a stream is a second function of the transport, in core, with the same three `fetch` options and `withDeadline` for the time to the first byte only. Direct mode: the bridge buffers a response, and says so in a notice. The echo server's `/sse` exists.
- [ ] **P5.7** WebSocket (direct). First check in 3 engines that `connect-src *` admits `ws:` and `wss:`; if it does not, add them through `security/csp.json` with a claim test. Then: connect with subprotocols, a message log (in/out, time, size), send text or JSON, close codes, and a notice that custom handshake headers need the bridge WebSocket proxy (follow-up).
  - AC: e2e `/ws` echo of 3 messages is logged in order in 3 browsers.
  - P5.0: the echo server's `/ws` exists. A WebSocket message that holds a `{{$secret.…}}` reference is a new place a value goes on the wire: `containsSecretPlaceholder` (C-007), and the log is a new place something is shown or kept (the redactor).
- [ ] **P5.8** (new in P5.0, as P4.10 was for Phase 4) Release v2.3.0: version, changelog, budgets reset, the docs read for the release, Appendix A's row, section 0 rewritten, the tag on the release PR's merge commit.
  - AC: `check:claims` passes; every finding listed against Phase 5 in section 12 is Closed or has a later task named.

**Exit criteria:**
- Tabs, runner, cookies (via the bridge), GraphQL, SSE and WebSocket pass their e2e tests in 3 browsers (cookies and redirect chain in Chromium plus the bridge job).

### Phase 6 — Local Bridge first-class and the CLI (2 sessions) → v2.4.0

**Goal:** everything the browser cannot do is one `npx` away, and collections run in CI.

**Deliverable:** `wayfarer-bridge` and `wayfarer-cli` on npm with provenance; bridge protocol v2; route policy.

**Tasks:**

- [ ] **P6.1** Move `local-bridge/` to `packages/bridge/`. Protocol v2 (section 4.5):
  - lossless base64 bodies;
  - raw header list with `Set-Cookie` separate (built in P5.3, in protocol 1: P5.0);
  - decompression of gzip, deflate and br (Node `zlib`) with both sizes reported;
  - timings from socket events (`lookup`, `connect`, `secureConnect`, first byte, end);
  - redirect chain with optional follow (max 20) (built in P5.4, in protocol 1: P5.0);
  - `Host` override allowed;
  - `tls.insecure` per request (the UI shows a red "TLS verification off" pill);
  - custom CA via `--ca <file>`;
  - honours `HTTP_PROXY`, `HTTPS_PROXY` and `NO_PROXY`;
  - `/health` returns `{version, protocol, capabilities}`.
  - Zero runtime dependencies kept.
  - AC: `node --test` suite extended with one test per capability; a gzip response decodes correctly; the `Set-Cookie` pair with a comma in `Expires` arrives as 2 entries.
- [ ] **P6.2** Bridge hardening:
  - the `Host` header must be a loopback name (DNS-rebinding defence): done in PR #147, with the token rules; keep its tests;
  - `Access-Control-Allow-Private-Network: true` on preflights that request it;
  - HTTPS loopback mode (required: P1.10 found WebKit blocks `http://127.0.0.1` from the HTTPS site as mixed content; see `docs/browser-limits.md#bridge-reachability`);
  - a pre-explained Local Network Access prompt (P1.10: Chrome 153 denies loopback until the user grants it);
  - a one-time pairing code shown in the terminal that the app exchanges for the token (no manual token copy);
  - the token is still revocable via `--rotate-token`.
  - AC: tests: a wrong `Host` gives 403; the PNA preflight header is present; pairing with a wrong code 3 times locks pairing for 60 s.
- [ ] **P6.3** Version handshake: the app reads `/health`. On a protocol mismatch it shows "Bridge vX is too old/new — run `npx wayfarer-bridge@latest`".
  - AC: a unit test with a mocked protocol-1 bridge shows the message.
- [ ] **P6.4** RoutePolicy (D7): per-request, collection and global settings `auto | direct | bridge`, and a route badge on every response ("direct" / "via bridge").
  - AC: unit tests for the 8 D7 cases; e2e: a POST with a JSON body (non-simple) against `/cors/none` auto-retries via the bridge; a simple POST form against `/cors/none` asks for confirmation instead of retrying.
- [ ] **P6.5** `packages/cli`: `wayfarer-cli run <collection|workspace-backup|postman.json> [-e env.json] [-d data.csv] [-n iterations] [--reporter cli,junit,json] [--bail] [--timeout ms] [--insecure]`. Uses the same `packages/core` (resolver, auth, QuickJS Node variant, runner), Node's built-in `fetch` (`undici` only if `--insecure` or proxy support needs a dispatcher), and the file-based vault via `--vault-file` plus the `WAYFARER_VAULT_PASSPHRASE` env var.
  - AC: the CLI run of the P5.2 fixture produces the same results JSON as the in-app runner (a CI diff test); exit code 1 on any failed test and 0 otherwise; runs on Node 22 and 24 in CI.
- [ ] **P6.6** Publish `wayfarer-bridge` and `wayfarer-cli` via `release.yml` with `npm publish --provenance`. `docs/cli.md` gets a GitHub Actions example.
  - AC: `npx wayfarer-bridge --help` and `npx wayfarer-cli --version` work from a clean machine; the npm page shows the provenance badge; `npm deprecate` is set on both `0.0.0-reserved` placeholders.

**Exit criteria:**
- `npx wayfarer-bridge` paired in under 60 s in the documented flow on 3 browsers (or the documented HTTPS alternative).
- The CLI matches in-app results.
- Both packages published with provenance.

### Phase 7 — Hardening and release gate (1 session) → v2.5.0

**Goal:** accessibility, performance, security and documentation are at a level where the claims can be defended publicly.

**Deliverable:** WCAG 2.2 AA pass, performance budgets, diagnostics, threat model, rewritten docs, SBOM and self-host tarball.

**Tasks:**

- [ ] **P7.1** Accessibility (text contrast at 4.5:1 in both themes and the both-themes axe sweep were done in PR #163; this task covers the rest):
  - axe scans for every dialog and view in 3 browsers (extend `e2e/accessibility.spec.ts`);
  - keyboard-only e2e of the core flow (new tab → URL → header → send → save → run);
  - visible focus;
  - focus returns to the trigger on dialog close;
  - `prefers-reduced-motion` respected;
  - screen-reader labels on all icon buttons.
  - AC: 0 axe violations at WCAG 2.2 AA tags in all scanned states; the keyboard e2e passes in 3 browsers.
- [ ] **P7.2** Performance:
  - Lighthouse CI on the prod-parity build with assertions: performance ≥ 90, LCP ≤ 2.0 s and TBT ≤ 200 ms (desktop preset);
  - a runtime check that typing into the URL bar with 5 tabs open stays at INP ≤ 100 ms (Playwright `PerformanceObserver` event timing);
  - Monaco, QuickJS, GraphQL, OpenAPI and YAML confirmed lazy (absent from the initial chunk list).
  - AC: Lighthouse assertions pass in CI; the initial bundle is within the P1.12 budget.
- [ ] **P7.3** Diagnostics view (local only):
  - app version and commit;
  - browser;
  - storage persisted and quota;
  - IDB schema version;
  - SW state and version;
  - bridge version and protocol;
  - self-tests (QuickJS `1+1`, vault crypto round-trip, IDB write/read/delete, same-origin fetch);
  - last 200 errors (redacted);
  - CSP violations;
  - "Copy report" (markdown) and "Open GitHub issue" (prefilled title and body, with URLs, headers and bodies excluded).
  - AC: e2e: a forced IDB error appears in the list; the copied report contains no string from a secret-bearing request sent earlier (redaction scan).
- [ ] **P7.4** Threat model `docs/threat-model.md` (STRIDE per trust boundary: page ↔ worker, page ↔ bridge, imported files, OAuth callback, SW, CDN). Each mitigation links to a claim ID or test.
  - AC: every boundary row has a mitigation with a linked `@claim` test; `check:claims` validates the links.
- [ ] **P7.5** Public "break the sandbox" challenge: `SECURITY.md` section with scope (script sandbox, vault, bridge), rules, and credit in a hall of fame in `docs/trust-center.md`. No money.

**Exit criteria:**
- All section 1 success criteria hold.
- Section 12 has no `Open` rows.
- Trigger the Phase 2 market re-analysis.

### Milestones

Sized in sessions since v1.2.0. One session is one sitting of an agent with the maintainer available to approve the PRs that wait for them; Phase 1.5 (planned at 12 engineering days) was done on 2026-10-06 and 2026-10-07. The engineering-day estimates made at lock are in git history.

| Milestone | Deliverable | Acceptance criteria | Sessions | State |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| M0 Truthful | Phase 0 | Phase 0 exit criteria | — | Done except owner actions |
| M1 Verified | Phase 1 | Phase 1 exit criteria | — | Done except owner actions |
| M1.5 Platform reset | v1.3.0 | Phase 1.5 exit criteria | — | Done, 2026-10-07 |
| M1.6 Material and hardening | v1.4.0 | P2.0 (d) | part of 2A | Merged, unreleased |
| M2 Correct core | v2.0.0 | Phase 2 exit criteria, R1 checkpoint recorded | 4 (2A, 2B, 2C, 2D) | Done, 2026-10-10 (4 sessions used) |
| M3 Scripts | v2.1.0 | Phase 3 exit criteria | 2 (3A, 3B) | Done, 2026-10-10 (2 sessions used; 3B in two sittings) |
| M4 Interop | v2.2.0 | Phase 4 exit criteria | 3 (4A, 4B, 4C) | Done, 2026-10-12 (3 sessions used; 4B in three sittings, 4C in two) |
| M5 Workspace | v2.3.0 | Phase 5 exit criteria | 3 (5A, 5B, 5C; planned at 2 until P5.0) | Begun 2026-10-12 |
| M6 Bridge + CLI | v2.4.0 | Phase 6 exit criteria | 2 | |
| M7 Gate | v2.5.0 | Phase 7 exit criteria; owner pass done; Phase 2 re-analysis started | 1 | |

Before a phase starts, its session re-reads the phase against the repository and splits it into sessions the way Phase 2 is split here. Phase 3 had that pass in P3.0 (v1.2.5), Phase 4 in P4.0 (v1.2.7) and Phase 5 in P5.0 (v1.2.32). Phases 6 and 7 have not had it beyond the corrections of v1.2.0.

## 10. Testing strategy

- **Unit (Vitest):**
  - `packages/core` in Node: resolver, redactor, importers, exporters, auth providers, runner engine, cURL parser, non-simple-request computation, inheritance.
  - App specs in Chromium browser mode: repositories and migrations with fixture DBs, vault v2, services, components.
  - Coverage gates: `src/app` lines ≥ 70% (live since P1.5.5), `packages/core` lines ≥ 90% (from P2.1). Mutation testing (Stryker, weekly scheduled plus on PRs touching the files) for `resolver.ts`, `redactor.ts` and the vault's pure crypto functions, with score ≥ 85%.
  - Tests that depend on time (idle lock, backup reminder, timeouts) use Playwright's clock or Vitest's fake timers, never a real wait.
- **Property-based (fast-check):** resolver idempotence and precedence, cURL round-trip, redaction completeness over random secrets and encodings, importer robustness (no throw other than `ImportError`, bounded time) on arbitrary JSON/YAML.
- **Conformance:** `pm-compat` suite (P3.3), SigV4 vectors (P4.8), Fetch "simple request" cases (P2.14), JUnit XSD validation (P5.2).
- **Integration / e2e (Playwright, 3 browsers, prod-parity headers, local echo-server only):**
  - tripwires;
  - `@claim` tests;
  - the escape suite;
  - per-feature specs listed in the task ACs;
  - multi-tab specs (two pages sharing one context);
  - migration spec (seed v4 DB via `page.evaluate`, then load v5 app);
  - offline and update SW spec.
- **Load / performance:** script benchmark gate (P3.10), 5,000-request import (P4.1), 60 MB response download (P2.3), Lighthouse CI (P7.2).
- **Security:** escape suite; redaction scans across all IDB stores and exports; CodeQL; Scorecard; `npm audit signatures`; HTML-preview network-silence test; OAuth state and origin checks.
- **Rollout strategy:** one release per phase. Features that change stored data (v5 migration, vault v2) ship with automatic pre-migration backups. Any feature not finished by its phase exit stays behind a `settings.experimental.<name>` flag, off by default, and is excluded from claims.
- **Rollback plan:**
  - web: `wrangler rollback` to the previous version (automated on smoke failure, P1.7);
  - data: IDB cannot downgrade, so rollback of a data-model release means shipping a hotfix forward; users can restore from the automatic pre-migration backup via Settings → Backups;
  - SW: deploy `sw-kill.js` (R12);
  - npm: `npm deprecate` the bad version and publish a patch.

## 11. Operations

- **Observability (local only):** diagnostics store (P7.3), CSP violation capture, bridge terminal log line per relay (method, host, status, ms — no headers or bodies).
- **Monitoring:** `synthetic.yml` every 6 h in 3 browsers; the deploy smoke gates every release.
- **Alerts:** a GitHub issue labelled `prod-down` (with repo notifications) from the synthetic workflow or a failed deploy smoke.
- **Runbook:** `docs/runbook.md` has the Cloudflare zone steps, the npm name reservation and the service worker kill switch; `docs/deployment.md` has rollback. Still to write: a broken migration hotfix (P2.19), yanking an npm release (P6.6), rotating the Cloudflare API token (P7.9).
- **On-call implications:** solo maintainer; response target for `prod-down` issues is 48 h, stated in `SECURITY.md` and the Trust Center.

## 13. Adversarial review log

Objections raised against draft 1 of this plan, and how the plan changed. Kept so later readers know why the plan is shaped this way.

1. **"Fixing secret resolution in Phase 0 is small — why wait until Phase 2?"** A quick fix would resolve plaintext secrets into `request.headers`. `RequestExecutionService` then persists that into history. The quick fix would therefore turn a harmless literal-placeholder bug into a plaintext credential leak. Phase 0 blocks the send instead (P0.3); the real fix ships together with redaction (P2.4 + P2.8).
2. **"Temporarily allow `unsafe-eval` on worker files to restore scripts fast."** That restores the deny-list sandbox this plan rejects. Also, without a stable file name the header would have to cover every `worker-*.js`, including Monaco's. Phase 0 disables scripts with a banner instead (P0.2).
3. **"Auto-fallback to the bridge on CORS failure is a nice UX win."** For CORS-simple requests the browser already sent the request before failing to read the response, so an automatic retry duplicates POSTs. D7 restricts auto-retry to preflighted or safe-method requests.
4. **"Taint tracking will catch every secret."** It does not survive concatenation, Basic-auth base64 or HMAC inputs. D5 adds output value-matching across encodings, plus header-name rules, and R5 adds mutation testing.
5. **"Cmd+T for new tab."** Browser tabs cannot intercept it. D9 picks non-reserved chords and AC P5.1 tests this in 3 browsers.
6. **"Use `ngsw-bypass` to stop the fake 504."** On cross-origin requests that header triggers CORS preflight and changes the user's request. Replaced by a same-origin-only custom SW (P1.6).
7. **"e2e with `wrangler dev` gives prod parity."** It is closer, but still not Cloudflare's edge. The real gate is the version-URL smoke before promotion (P1.7). The PR e2e uses the fast header-applying server (P1.1).
8. **"Claims ledger is paperwork."** It is only useful because CI enforces it in both directions (doc → test and test → doc, P1.4). Otherwise it would be dropped.
9. **"OAuth2 from the browser just works."** Many token endpoints have no CORS. R8 routes token calls through RoutePolicy and tests both paths.
10. **"Migration is low-risk; it's just reshaping JSON."** IDB versions cannot downgrade, and other open tabs block upgrades. P2.2 adds backups, Web Locks, `versionchange` handling and fixture DBs from every past version.
11. **"QuickJS will be fine for performance."** Unmeasured. P3.10 is a hard gate with a named mitigation (host-native crypto shims) decided in advance.
12. **"Scope is fine."** It is 83+ days for one person. R1 sets a checkpoint with a pre-agreed cut list (P5.5–P5.7), and every phase ships independently, so stopping early still leaves a correct product.
13. **"Import any Postman script and run it."** Imported scripts plus `pm.sendRequest` plus secret access form an exfiltration path. D6 and P3.8 gate untrusted scripts.
14. **"Delete the Trust Center, it's premature."** The goal is enterprise later, and the facts are cheap to keep true once they are test-backed. D12 trims instead of deleting.
15. **"Local Bridge HTTPS on loopback is overkill."** It is included only if spike P1.10 proves browsers block HTTP loopback. The spike runs in week 1 because R4 scores 9.

Second pass (draft 2 → draft 3):

16. **P0.4 used `responseType: 'text'`.** That still corrupts binary bodies before the download button can save them. Changed to `arraybuffer` plus `TextDecoder`.
17. **The P0.5 safety worker only helps clients that fetch it at the old path.** It is now emitted as `ngsw-worker.js` and kept there permanently (P1.6 AC e), so pre-v1.1.0 installs are cleaned up.
18. **The v5 migration ignored `history` and the reserved `RequestDoc.vars`.** Both are now migrated (section 4.4 steps 7–8), with a collision report instead of silent drops.
19. **The P2.10 AC depended on scripts, which are disabled until Phase 3.** Rewritten to use the environment editor plus a host-side unit test.
20. **Environment export still leaked dangling `$secret` references (F17).** P2.11 adds a 3-option export dialog with a safe default.
21. **Phase 2 had 17 tasks in 15 days, including the component split and all viewers.** Raised to 18 days, and the milestones were recomputed.
22. **The "no telemetry" claim had no test.** P0.8 now adds the `no-third-party-requests` `@claim` test.

Lock review (v1.0):

23. **"A hand-recorded Postman result is a weak oracle."** Newman is Postman's own open-source runtime. P3.4 and P4.2 now generate golden results by running Newman against the same local echo-server, so compatibility is measured against the reference implementation, not a snapshot someone recorded once.
24. **"Newman's fixtures call postman-echo.com, which breaks the no-internet rule for PR e2e."** The echo-server implements the Postman Echo routes those fixtures use, and fixture URLs are rewritten at load (P1.2).
25. **"Turning off zone settings once is not durable; someone can re-enable them."** P0.12 adds the `no-edge-injection` `@claim` test to the 6-hourly synthetic run, so a regression is caught within 6 hours.
26. **"The npm names are free today but not guaranteed tomorrow."** P0.13 reserves them in Phase 0 rather than at Phase 6 publish time.
27. **"Microsoft Graph's collection repo is archived."** It is used only as a frozen import fixture for OAuth2 and large-tree import. No behaviour depends on it being maintained.

Final review against the plan-quality checklist:
- goal observable (section 1);
- every task has a binary AC;
- ≥ 2 non-goals;
- 12 scored risks;
- real paths and symbols;
- behaviour-level tests;
- named dependencies;
- first task startable now (P0.1);
- open questions listed;
- no time-relative language.

Phase 1 execution review (PR #102):

28. **"Tag the tests that exist; untested bullets are marketing."** Rejected: every behavioural bullet got a test. That found a wire bug (F43), a history-delete popup bug and a false bullet ("raw view"). A claim ledger that skips features only checks the sentences that were easy to check.
29. **"Serve Postman Echo under a path prefix."** Rejected after review: a prefix changes `pm.request.url` and every path Newman's scripts assert on. A separate origin keeps them byte-identical.
30. **"Block service workers in e2e so routes are deterministic."** Kept only where a route needs it (the tripwires). The C-001 test uses a real echo target with the worker active, so it also covers requests the worker makes.

Re-baseline review (v1.2.0, 2026-10-09), made against `main` at `bcca26a`, GitHub, npm and production:

31. **"Every phase ships to production."** None has since Phase 0. Production serves a build from before Phase 1, with Cloudflare's injected script and NEL still on; deferring all Cloudflare work to the end (v1.0.3) turned six releases into one deploy that also migrates stored data. Added R16 and Q4; the migration's `blocked` path is designed for production's old tabs, which never close on request.
32. **"Split the composer last (P2.17)."** Six Phase 2 tasks add composer UI. Adding it to an 889-line component with a 628-line template and splitting afterwards is the mistake D14 was made to avoid. P2.17 moves to session 2A, and the v4 converters it needs are the ones the migration reuses.
33. **"Resolve secrets in P2.4, redact in P2.8, fix history in P2.9."** Any order that merges resolution first writes plaintext secrets into history, the exact leak item 1 describes. They are one PR now (D21). Vault v2 moves ahead of it: today each decrypt is a 600,000-iteration key derivation, so five secrets in one request would add seconds to every send.
34. **"Remove the P0.3 block" (P2.5).** `CLAUDE.md` and claim C-007 require that a `{{$secret.*}}` placeholder never reaches the network in any encoding. After resolution the check still has work: a deleted secret, "Send anyway". It stays; only its message changes.
35. **"Mask the secret's base64 form" (D5).** A secret inside a longer base64 string (`user:secret`) does not contain the base64 of the secret: the encoding depends on the offset modulo 3. The redactor matches all three alignments, and the JSON-escaped form. The property test places the secret at a random offset.
36. **"Scan the stores after sending a secret" (P2.8).** History v2 stores response bodies, and servers echo credentials (token endpoints, `/echo`). The test now sends to an endpoint that reflects the secret, so the response path is covered.
37. **"HTML preview in `<iframe srcdoc>`", "XML pretty".** `srcdoc` and `DOMParser.parseFromString` are Trusted Types HTML sinks, and since P1.5.9 the policy allows no HTML. Both would throw in production and pass under `ng serve`. D19 replaces them; the AC bans the sinks by grep and by violation listener, and adds meta refresh, stylesheet and link-click cases the old AC did not have.
38. **"The OAuth popup posts the code to `window.opener`" (P4.7).** PR #145 added `Cross-Origin-Opener-Policy: same-origin`; a popup that visits the identity provider comes back with no opener. The app also has no router to own `/oauth/callback`. D20: a static callback page and `BroadcastChannel`.
39. **"A verifier validates unlock" (D8).** Unwrapping the DEK with AES-GCM already fails on a wrong passphrase, so the verifier is a second ciphertext with no job. Removed. Added instead: the secret's id as additional authenticated data (two rows' ciphertexts cannot be swapped), a non-extractable session DEK, and a migration that computes everything before its one write (an IndexedDB transaction does not survive an `await` on WebCrypto).
40. **"Web Locks for migrations and environment writes."** An upgrade is an exclusive `versionchange` transaction by specification, and overlapping `readwrite` transactions are serialised across tabs. The locks added a second mechanism to get wrong. Removed (D22); the two-tab tests stay.
41. **"Snapshot all v4 stores into `backups` before transforming."** That doubles history inside the upgrade transaction, the likeliest quota failure, and the plan did not say what the user sees when the upgrade fails: today the app would fall back to memory-only mode and look empty. History is not copied; a failure screen offers the untouched data; the banner for "another tab upgraded" stops saying "reset"; old code meeting a v5 database gets its own message (section 4.4).
42. **"Free-text method" (P2.16).** The hardening after v1.3.0 made the importer reject any method outside seven verbs, as a defence for the cURL export. The two now agree on one rule (an RFC 9110 token), the export keeps quoting, and lower-case `patch`, which `fetch` does not normalise, is upper-cased.
43. **"N headers hidden by CORS when detectable" (P2.13).** It is never detectable: `Access-Control-Expose-Headers` is itself not exposed. Replaced by a fixed note on direct cross-origin responses.
44. **Smaller cuts.** `jsonpath-plus` (the assertion runner already walks paths); a virtualised JSON view (plain text over 5 MB); `scripts/check-file-size.mjs` (ESLint `max-lines` on the composer folder); a 510-send e2e in three engines (a cap of 5 in e2e, 500 in a unit test); a real 60-second idle wait (Playwright's clock).
45. **"New stores: `tabs`, `globals`, `cookies`, `files`, `backups`, `diagnostics`, `vault`."** Three of them serve Phases 5 and 7 and would be empty schema for months. v5 creates `files` and `backups`; globals and the vault record are rows in `meta`; later phases add their own store in their own upgrade.
46. **Tasks in the wrong phase.** P2.7 (scripts setting protected variables) and the script half of P2.15 need scripts to run; they move to Phase 3 as P3.12 and P3.9. The rest of P2.15 is one line of P2.3.
47. **"Coverage ≥ 90% for core, once core exists."** Code moved from `src/app` to `packages/core` leaves the 70% gate's denominator; without a gate on core from its first PR, moving code is a way to lose coverage unnoticed. P2.1 ships the gate. The "no DOM globals" rule is enforced by core's `tsconfig` (`lib` without `DOM`), not by a list of banned names.
48. **"Runs on Node 20 and 22" (P6.5), "Node ≥ 20.19" (bridge).** Node 20 reached end of life in April 2026. Node 22 and 24.
49. **"Input caps: 50 MB file" (P4.1).** `readImportText` and the shared validators refuse files over 10 MB, and `CLAUDE.md` makes that a rule. 10 MB stays; the 5,000-request fixture must fit under it.
50. **`crypto-js` is discontinued; `chai` 5 and later is ESM-only.** R18 for the first; P3.5 bundles the VM libraries with a build script, since "pre-bundled as strings" does not happen by itself.
51. **Playwright runs the Node test suites.** `e2e/support/*.test.mjs` match Playwright's default `testMatch`, so every e2e collection imports them and Node's test runner executes inside it. Harmless so far, but it starts servers during collection. F54, fixed in P2.0.
52. **"Phase 2 is one phase."** Seventeen tasks, three of which change stored data and need the maintainer, do not fit one session. D18 splits it at the points where `main` is coherent.
53. **D15 is not what the app is built on.** The maintainer chose Material on 2026-10-09. D16 records it; UI tasks in Phases 2 and 5 cite `CLAUDE.md` patterns (`mat-tab-nav-bar`, `mat-select` without a form field) instead of CDK primitives. The body modes also retire two editors (Q1).
54. **`connect-src *` and WebSocket (P5.7).** Whether `*` admits `ws:` and `wss:` differs by reading of the CSP specification. Unverified; P5.7 checks it in 3 engines before building, and adds `ws: wss:` through `security/csp.json` if needed.
55. **`Referer` on the user's requests.** With `Referrer-Policy: strict-origin-when-cross-origin` every request Wayfarer sends names Wayfarer's origin. D23 turns it off for user requests (Q2).
56. **Mixed content was not in the constraints.** An `http://` API cannot be called from the HTTPS site at all, which is a first-day surprise for anyone testing a local network service. Added to Constraints and to P2.14.
57. **The tracker and the tags drifted.** Tags `v1.1.0` and `v1.2.0` do not exist; five issues are open for closed findings; the changelog's Unreleased section has duplicate headings. P2.0 fixes what can be fixed and P0.11 is withdrawn.
58. **"Owner actions are all Cloudflare work."** Branch protection and the npm names are not, cost minutes, and protect against a wrong merge and a lost name today. Q3.

It makes v2.0.0 the first deploy with a migration instead of the first deploy at all (R16). **Answered 2026-10-09:** not done; and no backward compatibility is to be kept anywhere in this plan while the app has no users (D24). | Maintainer | Before 2B |

Should the app also stop and ask when a script changed the request's host and the request uses a vault secret? | No: scripts run only after a review, the docs say what approval means, and a check on `pm.request.url` alone would miss a host that comes from a variable, so it would promise more than it does. If yes, it belongs with P3.9, compares the host of the request as resolved with and without the script's changes, and needs a dialog and a claim. Raised in P3.3 (2026-10-10) by the automated review of the commit. **Answered 2026-10-10, before P3.9: yes.** Asked a second time with the limit spelled out (the comparison is of one send, so a host that an earlier script stored in a variable is not seen), the maintainer chose the one-send prompt with a narrow claim: C-053, built in P3.9 (section 16, v1.2.6). **Asked a third time for P4.9 (2026-10-10), which adds inherited scripts and folder variables:** the limit stays as documented; P5.2 looks at it again. | Maintainer | Before v2.1.0 is deployed |

## 15. Follow-up work (out of scope)

- Encrypted-workspace mode (collections, history and environments under the vault DEK).
- Bridge WebSocket proxy for custom handshake headers; gRPC via the bridge.
- File System Access workspace folders (Bruno-style files in Git) — Phase 2 wedge candidate.
- CORS debugger and encrypted repro links — Phase 2 wedge candidates, seeded by P2.14 and D5.
- mTLS client certificates in the bridge.
- Digest, NTLM, Hawk and OAuth 1.0 auth.
- Docker image for self-hosting.
- *(Moved into the plan in v1.1.0: the Angular upgrade and the P1.13 coverage gate for `src/app/**` are Phase 1.5, tasks P1.5.1–P1.5.5. `packages/core` ≥ 90% still starts with P2.1.)*
- A virtualised JSON view for very large responses; full JSONPath (filters, recursive descent) in the response filter.
- Dropping `style-src 'unsafe-inline'`: needs Monaco replaced or framed (section 16, v1.1.12).
- Per-document schema versions, if a later migration ever needs to run lazily instead of in the upgrade transaction.
- *(Left by Phase 2, decided in session 2D, each with its reason.)* **A history entry's `template` carries the `id`, `meta`, `name` and place of the saved request it was opened from**, because `WorkspaceStore.snapshot()` returns them. They are identifiers, not credentials, and the redactor has nothing to mask in them; opening the entry does not bind the composer to that request. Left until P5.1, which gives each open request its own state and decides what a snapshot is.
- **A workspace backup does not hold the files of request bodies.** A file may be 50 MB and an import is refused above 10 MB (`MAX_IMPORT_BYTES`), so a backup with files in it could not be restored. A restored request names its file and says so when sent without it. To change it, a backup would need a container format (a zip, or one file per body) and a streamed import: its own task, when someone asks for it.
- **The history list reads whole entries, bodies included** (at most 500 entries of at most 1 MB each). No slowness was measured in Phase 2. If P7.2's performance pass shows it, response bodies move to a second store read on demand.
- **F64 (#209):** confirm on real Safari whether a refused preflight is ignored while the service worker controls the page; if it is, say so in the composer's note for Safari or report it to WebKit.

| 2026-09-28 | v1.0 LOCKED | Maintainer answers applied: unscoped npm names reserved early (P0.13); Cloudflare zone hardening with Bot Fight Mode off zone-wide (P0.12); history bodies on and timeout 0 confirmed; plan archived at P7.8; Newman integration collections plus Adyen and Microsoft Graph chosen as fixtures, with Newman as the reference runner (P3.4, P4.2). Final review items 23–27 in section 13. |

Remove it when the builder handles this. **Tests changed, assertions unchanged:** C-031 (waits for the gutter to stop moving) and C-038 (axe waits for finite animations; PrimeNG 21 fades dialogs in with CSS). **Removed:** `src/polyfills.ts` (comment-only), `baseUrl` (deprecated in TypeScript 6). **Deprecation warnings left:** none; the build, unit and lint logs are clean. **Not run:** the optional `router-current-navigation` migration (the app never calls `Router.getCurrentNavigation`). **Migrations reverted:** `$safeNavigationMigration` wrappers and two suppressed extended diagnostics (neither was needed), and the removal of `tsconfig` `lib` (P1.11 keeps it explicit). **Coverage:** measured 50.91%, below the 70% target; raised to 70.71% with real tests instead of lowering the threshold. **F44** found and fixed (#106). **F45** found and left open (#109): not on the migration path. **Unit tests run isolated:** `angular.json` sets `"isolate": true` for the test target. With the builder's default (`false`), CI ran some spec files without the builder's TestBed reset hooks, so every test after the first in those files failed with "Cannot configure the test module when the test module has already been instantiated". It happened on every CI run, on Vitest 4 and 5, in 3 to 5 files that changed from run to run, and never locally (also not with caches cleared, `CI=true`, or under CPU load). A diagnostic run (throwaway PR #108) showed neither `beforeEach` nor `afterEach` from the setup file firing in the failing files. Isolated, CI passes 276 of 276 in 29 s. Cost: each spec file gets its own iframe. **Coverage gate proven in CI:** throwaway PR #108 raised the threshold to 99 and its Unit tests job failed with "Coverage for lines (70.74%) does not meet global threshold (99%)" (run 37553485655, job 112574203560). **Dependabot:** #45–#53 closed as superseded by #107; #55 stays open (monaco, Playwright, axe, autoprefixer, postcss, wrangler are not part of this phase), as do #41–#43 (Actions). `dependabot.yml` now ignores PrimeNG majors, so PrimeNG 22 is never proposed, and TypeScript minors and majors, which move with Angular. |
| 2026-10-07 | v1.1.2 | **F46, a regression shipped by Part A (#107) and fixed the next day (#111).** After the history drawer (or the phone navigation drawer) closed, its backdrop stayed in the page and blocked every click. All baseline commands had passed: no e2e test clicked the app after closing a drawer. It was found by capturing the same screens on the pre-upgrade commit (`0dfe14b`) and on `main` and comparing which states could be reached. Cause and fix: `provideAnimations()` routed PrimeNG 21's backdrop removal through Angular's animation renderer, which never flushed in a zoneless app; the provider and the `@angular/animations` package are removed, since neither the app nor PrimeNG 21 uses them. Three e2e tests now click the app after an overlay closes (2 of them failed in 3 engines before the fix). Lesson applied to Part B: every overlay slice gets an "app is usable after it closes" test, and each slice's screens are compared with the pre-upgrade set before its PR merges. Side effect: the initial bundle drops to 1,691,388 B raw / 437,719 B gzip, below the Phase 1.5 baseline. The C-016 test now waits for an overlay's fade-in to end before pressing Escape (PrimeNG 21 binds Escape after the fade); its assertions are unchanged. |
