| 2026-10-07 | v1.1.12 | P1.5.16 as executed: **`style-src 'unsafe-inline'` cannot be dropped, and the directive is unchanged.** Method: `style-src 'self' 'report-sample'` through `security/csp.json` and `npm run gen:csp`, a production build, and a `securitypolicyviolation` listener across a session (load, Settings dialog, history drawer, method select, a send with the JSON response, the Scripts tab) in Chromium, Firefox and WebKit; the CSP files were then restored. All three engines reported the same sources. **Still needing inline styles:** (1) Monaco (`monaco-editor`, response body, scripts, environment JSON): dozens of `style-src-attr` violations per editor (`top:0px;height:19px;line-height:19px;`, `width:217px;`, `left:0px;width:3.9375px`, one per rendered line and layer) and a `style-src-elem` for its codicon sheet (`.codicon-add:before{content:'\ea60'…`); Monaco writes these as attribute strings, so there is no setting that avoids them. (2) Angular's component styles, injected as `<style>` elements: 8 components (`[_nghost-…]{display:block…`, `.address-bar[_ngcontent-…]`, `.usage-chip`, `.shortcut-kbd` and others). (3) 13 static `style="…"` attributes in templates (`app-shell` ×4, `response-viewer` ×3, `collections-sidebar` ×2, and one each in `api-params`, `settings`, `environments-manager`, `past-requests`), for example `transition: margin-left var(--dur-enter) var(--ease-standard)` and `font-variation-settings: 'FILL' 0, 'wght' 200…`. (4) The CDK's own overlay and dialog styles, injected as `<style>` elements (`.cdk-overlay-container, .cdk-global-over…`, `.cdk-dialog-container{display:block…`, `.cdk-visually-hidden`). (2) to (4) could be moved into global CSS, but (1) cannot, and a static site has no per-response nonce, so the directive stays; hashing would need Monaco's attributes hashed too (`unsafe-hashes` per attribute value, which is not workable for computed positions). **What did get tighter in this phase:** nothing in the CSP itself; the reason in `security/csp.json` was rewritten (it named PrimeNG), `createHTML` left the Trusted Types policy in slice 2, and no `innerHTML`, `bypassSecurityTrust*` or `eval` was added (grep below). No claim was added: there is no new guarantee to claim. A maintainer who wants this closed later can (a) move 2 to 4 into global CSS and (b) replace Monaco or sandbox it in a same-origin iframe with its own CSP; neither is part of this phase. |

Deviations: (1) `safe-json.spec.ts` had one test that calls the app's two importers, which core cannot import; it stays in `src/app` as `shared/json/import-limit.spec.ts`, assertions unchanged, and core's spec holds the other two plus two new ones (the rethrow in `stringifyJson`, `isOversizedImport`). (2) `knip.json` is rewritten around `workspaces` (knip asks for that once the root has workspaces) instead of gaining a `packages/**/*.ts` pattern. (3) The lockfile is written by the npm that ships with Node 24 (11.17), as the existing one was: npm 10 strips the `libc` fields of 24 optional packages. `npx npm@10 ci` installs it. (4) The Core job also runs `tsc --noEmit` for core, since the missing DOM types are half of what keeps the DOM out. (5) `index.ts` is excluded from core's coverage (re-exports only). **P2.17** (#179): `ApiParams` is replaced by `app-composer` and ten small components in `src/app/components/composer/` over a `Draft` held by `WorkspaceStore`; `Draft`, `from-v4.ts` and `to-v4.ts` are in core. No behaviour change: 192 screenshots (390, 820 and 1440 px, both themes, three engines, eleven states each on desktop and ten on a phone) have 0 pixels of difference from `main`, twice. Deviations and decisions: (1) **the Basic body editor's rows stay beside the draft.** The draft holds the body as JSON text, which has no place for a row whose name is still empty; `WorkspaceStore.bodyRows` is that editor's state and the draft's body is rewritten from it on every change. P2.12 removes the rows editor (Q1). (2) **`ComposerView`**, an injectable provided by the composer, holds what the panels share that is not the request: the open tab, Basic or JSON mode, the JSON editors' text. The plan named only the store. (3) The panels are address row, params, headers, body, auth, scripts and tests as planned, plus `variable-chips` and `save-as-dialog`, which the 250-line template limit needed. (4) `api-params.spec.ts` (24 tests) became `composer.spec.ts` (14) and `composer-request.spec.ts` (10) with a shared setup in `src/testing/composer-setup.ts`, because of `max-lines`. Every expected value is unchanged; what is asserted on moved to the new owner (`component.requestBody()` is `store.bodyRows()`, `component.requestAuth()` is `authToV4(store.draft().auth)`). (5) `Draft.params` is not filled from a v4 request's reserved `params`, and `to-v4` writes none: the composer's rows mirror the URL, as before; the migration (P2.2) decides about the reserved field. (6) The identity property holds for requests as the v4 composer writes them (header names trimmed and not empty, the chosen auth type's fields present). Two shapes are normalised when a request is loaded and saved again, with no effect on what is sent: an auth type chosen with no field typed is saved as `{type: "bearer", bearer: {token: ""}}` where it was `{type: "bearer"}`, and fields of an auth type no longer chosen are not written back. `DB_VERSION` is still 4 and every stored document still validates as v4. A body value of `-0` is `0` after a load and save, since the draft holds JSON text (found by fast-check in CI: the generator now passes bodies through JSON text, and its seed is fixed). (7) `RequestBody` has the modes none and raw only, and `AuthConfig` the four types of today; the rest of section 4.4 arrives with the task that uses it. (8) `HttpAuthPlaceholder`, `HTTP_METHODS` and the assertion types are now defined in core and re-exported by `src/app/models`. (9) The screenshot comparison first used full-page captures and was not repeatable on `main` itself; that is F55. With a 2200 px viewport and no full-page capture it is. **Found:** F55 (#177) and F56 (#178). Initial bundle 1,153,088 B raw / 287,953 B gzip (+1,359 B raw: eleven components where there was one). **P2.3** (#180): `FetchTransport` and `BridgeTransport` in core behind `TransportRouter`; `HttpTransport`, `provideHttpClient` and every `@angular/common/http` import are gone; Cancel, the timeout setting (`wayfarer:request-timeout-ms`), the redirect note and the Blob path above 50 MB are in. Q2 taken on its default: no `Referer`. Deviations and decisions: (1) **not `AbortSignal.any`.** As specified (`AbortSignal.any([signal, AbortSignal.timeout(ms)])`) the timeout never ended a request in WebKit: the e2e test failed 3 of 3 there and passed in Chromium and Firefox, while the same two calls in a page script that kept a reference to the signal worked. `withDeadline` uses one controller and one timer; 45 of 45 repeats pass in three engines. (2) `ResponseEnvelope.charset` is added: bridge protocol 1 returns text already decoded, so its bytes are UTF-8 whatever the target's Content-Type says. It goes with protocol 2 (P6.1). (3) `TransportError` carries the bridge's own status, which the response panel showed before and still shows. (4) `ResolvedRequest.body` is `string | ArrayBuffer | Blob`: the Node types core compiles against have no `BodyInit`. (5) `response-body.ts` (`decodeResponseBody`, `BinaryBody`) moved to core with its spec, so the transports' tests could keep their assertions; `BinaryBody` may hold a `Blob`. (6) `TransportRouter` adds `Content-Type: application/json` to a body sent without one, on both routes: HttpClient did that for the direct route, and `fetch` would say `text/plain`. Through the bridge this header is new. (7) The texts HttpClient produced are kept where a user or the history saw them: `Http failure response for <url>: <status> <text>` for an error status with no body and in a history entry's `error`. (8) A timeout or a cancel has no status code, is recorded in history with its message, and still runs the Tests-tab assertions (against status 0). (9) Ported tests: the six bridge tests are in `packages/core/src/transport/bridge.spec.ts` and the direct-route tests in `fetch.spec.ts`, against a fake `fetch`. Expected values are unchanged; three things had to change with the interface: a non-2xx status is asserted on the response, not on a thrown error; a bridge failure's text is `error.message` (was `error.error`); `responseType === 'arraybuffer'` became `body instanceof ArrayBuffer`. In `request-executor.spec.ts` one matcher changed, `toBe(png)` to `toEqual(png)`: the executor decodes the bytes itself now, so the body is equal, not identical. (10) No claim is added here: the claims table gives P2.3 a new claim for cancel and timeout, and a new public claim waits for the maintainer, so it is its own small PR. The two limitation bullets (#67, #68) are removed from README and the Trust Center. (11) The budgets are reset: the bundle fell by 19,015 B raw. **Fixed:** F56 (test first: the header list lost `__proto__` on the old loop). **Flake registered:** Firefox went away mid-test once in CI on #179 (`e2e/FLAKES.md`). |
| 2026-10-09 | v1.2.2 | **Session 2B as executed**, one entry per PR. **Maintainer answers:** Q1 by independent review (below, P2.12); Q4 not done, and "do not worry about backward compatibility, make breaking changes, we have no active users" for every task of the plan; Q5 merge on green CI. Asked what the Q4 answer meant for P2.2, the maintainer chose "no migration": **D24**. **P2.2:** rewritten around D24 before any code. Section 4.4's migration design, R2, R16, the C-026 row, the exit criteria of 2B and of Phase 2, and the mentions of the backup copy in P2.11 and of the migration note in P2.19 are changed with it. As built: (1) `runUpgrade` deletes the stores an older version left and creates the v5 stores; a one-time banner says older data was removed (`CLAUDE.md`: never silently). (2) The request model is stored: `RequestDoc` is `RequestContent` plus its place; headers and params are rows; the body is text; auth is `AuthConfig`; `scripts` replaces the two script fields. `AuthEditor` edits `AuthConfig` directly. (3) Environment variables are rows. The environments manager still edits name and value pairs and its JSON view still shows an object, so a duplicate name or a switched-off row from a file is flattened if that environment is edited in the JSON view. (4) **Scope held back to what something uses** (`CLAUDE.md`: no scaffolding): only `scriptTrust.trusted` (the hash is P3.12's), no collection or folder variables, auth or scripts, no `files` or `backups` store, no `globals` or `vault` record, no new auth types or body modes; section 4.4 lists which task brings each. (5) **History is unchanged**: it keeps the v4 record until P2.9, which owns history v2; the migration was the only reason P2.2 touched it. A history entry is turned into a request when it is loaded (`requestFromHistory`). (6) `Draft.params` is stored as composed, and the URL stays the truth: opening a request reads the rows from its URL, as before. (7) Saving: names of rows are trimmed and rows with no name are left out (the editor's blank row); a switched-off row is kept. Save As writes the whole request in one call where it made two. (8) Format 2: `$id` first; trust is not written to a file and an import is untrusted, also over a trusted collection; the environments file is `{$id, environments}` and a bare array is refused. (9) The validator is in core (`validateRequestContent`, `validateRows`) and reads own fields only. `fast-check` stays in core for a property that every request the model allows passes it. (10) **Tests whose claim changed, changed with it:** `v4.spec.ts` went with the converters (its `__proto__` header and auth-shape cases live on in `composer-request.spec.ts` and `request.spec.ts`); the two upgrade tests that asserted v1 and v2 data survives now assert it is removed; C-026 runs on a format 2 tree with duplicate headers, a switched-off row, unsorted assertions and a body that is not JSON; environment-file tests expect the wrapper and the new messages; `confirmSaveAs` asserts the one write carries the whole request and that no second write happens. Fixtures across the specs and `seedAndOpen` are written in the stored shape through `src/testing/request-fixtures.ts`; no expected value of a request sent or shown changed. (11) No screenshot comparison: the only template changes are bindings in `auth-editor.html` and banners that show in the new states. **Found:** F57 (#182) and F58 (#183), both fixed test first. The Q1 review listed seven more defects in the two editors (a non-object JSON body is silently ignored and Send is not blocked; a number edited in a row becomes a string; opening JSON mode adds a `Content-Type` row; `Content-Type` cannot be removed in Basic; changing to a method without a body discards the body; `{{var}}` in a body key is not resolved; `{}` cannot be written as a body): they go with the editors in P2.12 and are checked there. Initial bundle 1,136,533 B raw / 283,416 B gzip (+2,460 B raw over session 2A); budgets unchanged. **P2.12:** **Q1 was decided by an independent adversarial review**, as the maintainer asked: drop both editors. Its reasons: the rows editor for a JSON body can only hold text (a row holding `42` is sent as `"42"`) and what it can express is exactly a URL-encoded form, which now has its own rows; the headers JSON view cannot hold order, a name twice or a switched-off row, and it forced a `Content-Type` row. Its strongest counter-argument (a JSON body becomes Monaco-only) lost because rows remain for headers, forms and multipart. As built: (1) `RequestBody` is `{mode, raw?, urlencoded?, multipart?, binary?}` as section 4.4 has it: every part is kept and `mode` says which is sent, so changing the mode loses nothing. No `graphql` (P5.5). (2) **`DB_VERSION` is 6**: the `files` store. A version 5 database keeps its data (`runUpgrade` creates the store and nothing else); only a database below 5 is emptied. (3) **Files:** a picked file is held in memory by `RequestFiles` until its request is saved, then written to `files` in the request's transaction, so an abandoned draft leaves nothing. `sweepFiles` deletes a file once no saved request names it and runs in every transaction that removes or rewrites requests (delete request, folder, collection; update with a body; import). A copy of a request shares its files. It reads every request each time; a reference count is the upgrade if that shows. A request imported elsewhere names a file that browser lacks, and the send says so. The store holds a file's bytes and type, not a `Blob`: with a `Blob`, saving the request failed in Playwright's WebKit and passed in the other two engines. (4) **`Content-Type` comes from the body, in the store**, unless a header row sets one; `TransportRouter` no longer adds `application/json` (deviation 6 of P2.3 is withdrawn). A new request has no `Content-Type` row, the row is no longer locked, and a POST with no body sends none (it sent `{}`). (5) Variables in a raw body are substituted in the text, not in the parsed JSON's strings, so `{"n": {{count}}}` works and a value is not JSON-escaped (as in Postman). Send is not blocked on text that is not valid JSON: it is sent as typed; Monaco underlines it. (6) Building a request is synchronous unless a file is read, so a request without files still leaves in the same task as the click (the unit tests assert that). (7) History keeps a text body as the text sent; a file or form body is not recorded, and cURL and HAR are written without it. History v2 is P2.9. (8) The Local Bridge refuses a body that is not text (protocol 1 would relay `{}`); protocol 2 is P6.1. (9) `containsSecretPlaceholder` also reads a form body percent-decoded and every name, value and file name of a multipart form; a file's bytes are the user's and are not read. (10) Header rows gained the "send" switch the model has, and **Bulk edit** is a plain `<textarea>` (`Name: value` per line, `#` for a row kept but not sent). (11) **Layout:** the switch took room from the fields, and "at 768 px…" and "at 1220 px…" failed (a header's name field under 80 px). Rows now wrap by their own room (`flex-wrap`, `flex-basis`), as `CLAUDE.md` asks; both tests pass unchanged. The Key and Value column headings went (placeholders say it). (12) Monaco: the XML and HTML grammars are registered (no service, no worker); `JsonEditor` takes a `language`. (13) **Tests whose claim changed:** C-039 is reworded and its test now checks every method and lists the modes; the keyboard test of the Basic / JSON switch drives Bulk edit and the body type instead; `key-value.spec.ts` went with `key-value.ts`; the composer tests that built a body from rows build it as JSON text, with the sent value asserted as before and history's body asserted as text; "manages dynamic header and body rows" keeps its header half and gains "the body is kept when the method changes"; the router test that asserted the added `Content-Type` asserts none is added, and the composer asserts the type per language; the transport e2e fills header row 1 where it added a row 2 after the default one. `FetchMock` gives a text body that is not JSON as text. (14) No before/after screenshot comparison: the composer is meant to look different; screenshots at 390 and 1440 px were read, which is how the remove button standing alone on a wrapped line was found and fixed. **The seven editor defects the review listed** are gone with the editors; "the body is kept when the method changes" and "sends raw text as written" hold two of them as tests. Closes #65 (F08). **P2.16:** as specified, with these decisions. (1) **A text field and a menu, not an autocomplete.** Material's autocomplete was built first and added 23,347 B raw (6,592 B gzip) to the initial bundle for one field; a text field with a "Common methods" menu beside it (the menu is already in the bundle) added 1,820 B. (2) The field itself is upper-cased as it is typed, caret kept: through the draft alone, typing `patch` over `PATCH` left the field showing what was typed (found by the C-017 test). (3) `isHttpMethod` is in core: an RFC 9110 token of at most 32 characters, in upper case, and it is the importer's check; a file with `get` is refused, as the app never writes one. (4) `CONNECT`, `TRACE` and `TRACK` are refused before the send when the Local Bridge is off, with the reason; with it on they are relayed. They can be saved and imported. (5) An empty or malformed method is refused before the send with what a method is. (6) The "New request" form in the sidebar keeps its list of seven: a request made there is opened in the composer, where any method can be typed. (7) No stored shape changes: `method` was already text. (1) **The message is `{stores}`, not `{store, ids}`.** A listener reads the whole store again (a few hundred documents at most), so ids would be carried and not used. `IdbCore.commitOrRollback` announces the stores of its transaction after it commits; history's three writes, which do not go through it, announce themselves. (2) Listeners: `CollectionsStore` (collections, folders, requests), `EnvironmentsStore` (environments, and `meta` for the active one), `App` (history). (4) After a save the editor shows what is stored, the other tab's variables included; a change made elsewhere to the open environment is shown at once unless the editor holds edits that are not saved. (5) Tests first: against the code before this PR, "what one tab saves appears in the other…" failed on the first check and "two tabs save different variables…" stored `base` and `fromB` without `fromA`. A first draft of the second test filled the row before the new one was drawn and failed for that reason instead; its helper now waits for the row. The repository test makes the two changes without awaiting the first. The e2e passes 30 of 30 (5 repeats, two tests, three engines). | 2026-10-09 | v1.2.3 | **Session 2C as executed**, one entry per PR. **Maintainer answers** (the prompt's two answers were left as placeholders; asked before P2.6): Q5, merge on green CI, as in 2B; D24 still holds, there are no users and breaking changes are fine. **Carried over from 2B, both confirmed with a failing test first.** **F59** (#188): the environments editor rebuilt its rows from the JSON view. The JSON editor reports the text it is given as a change, so every edit of a row, and opening an environment, replaced the rows with the object's entries: a new row without a name went, a switched-off variable came back switched on, and of two rows of one name the first went. Fix: the JSON view shows the variables the rows give (enabled rows, by name); the text the rows produced is ignored when it comes back; an edit typed in the JSON view is applied with `variableChanges` and `applyVariableChanges`, so only the rows of the names it changed are touched. The 2B note that the JSON view "flattens" a file's rows no longer holds. **F60** (#190): `deleteFolder` deleted the folder and its own requests and left a folder inside it pointing at it. Decision: the folder inside is **not** deleted with it, as the 2B note assumed it should be. The sidebar shows every folder of a collection side by side, so the user deleting one folder sees another, unrelated-looking folder beside it; deleting that too would lose requests they did not select. It moves up to where the deleted folder was, in the same transaction. Nested folders shown as a tree, and deleting a subtree, belong to the importers (Phase 4). **P2.4:** as specified, with these decisions. (1) **The resolver works on text, not on a `Draft`, and its secret callback is synchronous.** Section 4.5 has `resolve(template: Draft, scopes, secrets: (id) => Promise<…>)` returning a `ResolvedRequest`. As built, `VariableResolver` (`packages/core/src/variables/resolver.ts`) is made once per request from a `ScopeStack` and a callback, `resolve(text)` is called for each text, and `taint`, `unresolved` and `lockedSecrets` are sets on it that describe the whole request. Reasons: the request is assembled in the app (files through `RequestFiles`, `FormData`, the auth header, URL normalising), which core cannot hold without the DOM types; and a request with nothing to wait for must leave in the same task as the click (the composer tests assert that). P2.5 reads the secrets first: a pass with no callback lists the ids in `lockedSecrets`, the vault gives them, and the second pass has them. Section 4.5 is to be corrected in the P2.5 PR, which is the one that uses the callback. (2) One error, `VariableNestingError`, for a circle and for more than 10 steps; its message names the chain and is shown under the address bar; nothing is sent. A chip for such a variable shows the message and does not throw. (3) `$randomInt` is 0 to 1000 and `$randomAlphaNumeric` one character, as in Postman. A dynamic name and `$secret.` are read before the scopes; any other `$name` is an ordinary variable. (4) The scopes `local` and `data` are in the resolver and nothing fills them yet (scripts in Phase 3, the runner in Phase 5); the composer's never-filled "request variables" field is deleted. (5) **`DB_VERSION` is 7 and nothing is emptied**, which is less than D24 allowed: the upgrade writes `variables: []` into each stored collection inside the upgrade transaction. The globals are the `meta` record `globals`; a database without it has none. `changeGlobals` and `changeCollectionVariables` apply `VariableChange`s to the stored rows inside one transaction and announce their store; `EnvironmentsStore` already listened to `meta` and `CollectionsStore` to `collections`. (6) A collection file must have `collection.variables` (checked with `validateRows`); the `$id` stays `wayfarer/collection/2`, since no release has written format 2 yet. (7) **One editor, `VariablesDialog`**, for both: opened by "Global variables" in the Environments panel and by "Variables" in a collection's menu. It has no "send" switch, like the environments editor: a save is a list of sets and removals, which cannot say "kept but off". A switched-off row that came from a file is shown and kept, and cannot be switched on there. A variable cannot be made a secret in this editor; a `{{$secret.<id>}}` typed as its value is resolved like any other. (8) The collection scope is the collection of the saved request the composer is bound to; a request not saved yet has none. (9) An unresolved `{{x}}` is still sent as written and listed under "Missing"; blocking it is P2.5. Secret references are blocked as before. Closes #87 (F30) except inheritance (P4.9). **F61** (#192), found by the unit run: see section 12; registered in `e2e/FLAKES.md` and left open. Two e2e tests that list the collection menu's items gained "Variables" (`keyboard.spec.ts` asserts the seven names; `features.spec.ts` waits for seven items). **P2.6:** vault v2 as D8 has it, with these decisions. (1) **No migration of v1 secrets** (D24; the maintainer confirmed for this session that vault v2 may empty the store). `DB_VERSION` is 8: the upgrade clears `secrets` and leaves everything else. The task's third bullet, its progress indicator and the two migration ACs are withdrawn and struck through in section 9; in their place a unit test opens a version 7 database holding a secret and finds none, the vault record absent and the notice set. The one-time notice now says what was removed (`IdbCore.clearedOldData` is `"all"`, `"secrets"` or `null`), and is shown for secrets only when there were any. A variable that named a removed secret keeps its reference. (2) **The cryptography is in core** (`packages/core/src/vault/vault-crypto.ts`: `createVault`, `unwrapDek`, `rewrapDek`, `encryptSecret`, `decryptSecret`, `validateVaultFile`), pure, for the CLI and for the mutation testing P2.8 asks for. `SecretCrypto` is deleted; `SecretsVault` holds the data key as a signal. Core names the key type through `importKey` (`VaultKey`): the Node types have no `CryptoKey` type. (3) `wrappedDek` is one base64url string, the 12-byte IV then the wrapped key; an envelope is `{v: 2, iv, ct}` with no `alg`. (4) The data key is generated extractable, wrapped, and then unwrapped as a key that cannot be exported; that second key is the one kept. A change of passphrase unwraps it extractable for the length of the call. (5) **Idle time locks the idle tab only; a lock by the user locks every tab.** D8 says a lock in any tab locks all. Applied to the idle timer, a tab left in the background would lock the tab being worked in every 15 minutes, since each tab counts its own key presses. (6) Two tabs choosing a passphrase at once: the first write is kept (`writeVault` reads inside its transaction), the second tab is told and asked for that passphrase. (7) **Vault file** `wayfarer/vault/2`: the record and the envelopes as stored, so an export decrypts nothing; it asks for the passphrase to confirm the user knows what opens the file. Import validates every field, accepts 600,000 to 10,000,000 iterations (a file must not make the page derive for minutes), decrypts under the file's key and encrypts under this vault's, keeps ids, and writes all secrets in one transaction or none. (8) The three actions are an inline form in the Secrets dialog (`VaultActions`), not dialogs on a dialog. (9) The short-secret warning is shown in the environments editor after a value under 6 characters is protected; `SHORT_SECRET_LENGTH` moves to the redactor in P2.5. (10) **Claims:** C-003's sentence still holds and is unchanged (its test reads the v2 envelope); C-004 and C-005 are reworded; C-043 (change of passphrase) and C-044 (vault file) are new, C-042 being taken by the open PR #181. 43 claims. The limitation bullets for #66 are gone from README and the Trust Center. (11) **Tests whose claim changed:** `secret-crypto.spec.ts` (5 tests) became `vault-crypto.spec.ts` in core; `secrets-vault.spec.ts` (14 tests over mocks of the deleted class) is rewritten over the real stores and WebCrypto, each old case kept under a title that still says it, except "treats an empty vault as first-use: unlocks with whatever passphrase is given", which asserted F09 and is now "refuses a wrong passphrase with no secret stored"; the C-027 e2e ended by expecting the creation dialog again after a lock and now expects the unlock dialog; the C-003 e2e read `envelope.alg`. Closes #66 (F09). **F62** (#195), found by a flake in the P2.6 run of `multi-tab.spec.ts` and fixed in its own PR, test first: after a save the environments editor shows the store's copy of the environment, not the one the save returned. The spec's store stub now reads the environments again when a change returns, as the store does. **F61 fixed** in its own PR, test first, before P2.5: `IdbCore` passes a "stores changed" message to its listeners only while it has the database open. It also blocked a new test of P2.5: within one spec file, the store of an earlier test heard the writes of a later one, opened the database again, and the later test's reset then failed with "Close other Wayfarer tabs". **P2.5 with P2.8 and P2.9**, one PR (D21). **Redactor (P2.8)**, `packages/core/src/redact/redactor.ts`: (1) It is given the plaintexts to look for and masks each as `***` in every form it can take: as text; percent-encoded as a component (upper and lower case hex), as a form field, and as a browser writes it into the query and the path of a URL; JSON-escaped, also with `\u` escapes; and inside base64 and base64url at the three positions a value can start in. Longest form first. A value under 6 characters is not looked for. (2) **What it is given** is wider than the vault's taint set: also the Auth tab's credentials as resolved, and the value of every credential header as sent. The last was added when the C-008 e2e found an `X-Api-Key` typed into a header row unmasked in the echoed response body that history kept. (3) A credential header (`Authorization`, `Proxy-Authorization`, `Cookie`, `Set-Cookie`, `X-API-Key`, any name with token, secret, key or pass) is masked whole. In a request as composed, a credential typed by hand is masked and one that holds a `{{variable}}` is kept: it is a reference. (4) The browser's URL forms were added when the scan e2e sent a secret in a query: the echo-server reported the URL as the browser had escaped it, which is neither the plain text nor `encodeURIComponent`. (5) **Mutation testing** runs through Stryker's command runner (`npm -w packages/core run mutation`, workflow `mutation.yml` on PRs that touch the three files and weekly). The Vitest runner of Stryker 10 does not switch mutants on under Vitest 5 (6.67% killed, all survivors untouched); the command runner starts Vitest once per mutant and takes 3 minutes. Scores: `redactor.ts` 91.43%, `resolver.ts` 97.10%, `vault-crypto.ts` 87.73%, 90.99% together; the build fails under 85%. Stryker brought `qs` 6.15.1 (three moderate advisories) through `typed-rest-client`; an `overrides` entry pins 6.16.0, and Stryker is a root dev dependency because npm 11 did not apply the override under the workspace. `npm audit` is 0. **History v2 (P2.9):** (6) `DB_VERSION` 9. History is removed, not converted (D24): an old entry holds `Authorization` as sent. The store is made again with one index. The one-time notice lists what was removed (`clearedOldData` is `"all"` or a list). (7) Record as section 4.4 has it, corrected there: `template`, `sent` with `bodyPreview`, `response`, `route`, `durationMs`, `error`. **A binary response body is not kept** (4.4 had `base64`): bytes cannot be searched for a secret. **A file or multipart body:** the template keeps the body as composed, file references included; `sent.bodyPreview` holds field names, file names, sizes and types, never bytes. The file itself may be gone when the entry is opened (it is swept when no saved request names it), and the send says so. (8) Cap 500 (Settings, 1 to 5000), enforced in the transaction that writes an entry; a setting keeps no response bodies; a body is masked first and cut to 1 MB after. Search is in memory over the loaded entries, by words: each must be in the URL, or be the method or the status. The list reads whole entries, bodies included; a second store for bodies is the step to take if that shows. (9) Opening an entry loads its template (scripts and tests included, where it kept the composer's own before) and shows the recorded response. **Guards (P2.5):** (10) A request is resolved twice only when it uses a secret: the first pass lists the ids, the vault is asked (the shell opens the unlock dialog on `SecretsVault.unlockRequested`; closing it sends nothing, D4), and the second pass has the values. A request without a secret is still built in the task of the click. All of it runs inside `buildRequest`, so after the pre-request script once scripts run again. (11) D3: a request with a `{{variable}}` without a value throws `UnresolvedVariablesError`; the composer shows which and offers "Send anyway"; a setting switches the guard off. (12) The wire check (C-007) is unchanged apart from its message, and "Send anyway" does not pass it. With no vault at all, a request that names a secret gets the dialog that makes one, and is then refused because the vault does not have that secret. **Exports:** (13) Export → Copy as cURL and Copy as HAR mask by default and have a "with credentials" twin each; the composer's Copy as cURL is a menu of two and never reads the vault (a reference stays as written); a collection's Export masks and "Export with credentials" does not. A vault secret is masked in every one of them. (14) **Environment export is not changed here.** P2.8 lists it, but a protected value is a reference, not a plaintext, and P2.11 gives environment export its three choices; masking plain variables by name now would be undone there. **Claims:** C-007, C-008 and C-014 reworded; C-045 (unresolved variables) new; 44. Limitation bullets for #60, #71 and #72 removed. **Tests whose claim changed:** the C-007 tripwire (dialog, cancel, then the missing secret); C-008 and C-014 (masked, and the opt-in); C-020 and the two C-035 e2e (menu item by exact name); the composer's "leaves an unresolvable {{var}}…" (held back first, then sent as written); "loads a history entry as what was sent, keeping the scripts and tests being composed" is now "…as the request it was composed as"; history fixtures across nine specs use `historyEntry()`; `findByUrl` went with its index and its test became the cap test. **Found on the way, in the tests themselves:** the scan's base64 reader first cut a run to a multiple of four characters and so could not see a secret at the end of one; fixed in the unit oracle and the e2e scanner before either was relied on, and each now has a test that it finds a secret at every offset. **Known and left:** a history entry's `template` also carries the `id`, `meta`, `name` and place of the saved request it was opened from, because `WorkspaceStore.snapshot()` returns them (it did before this PR). Closes #60 (F03), #71 (F14), #72 (F15), #64 (F07). **P2.11:** as specified, with these decisions. (1) **A restored collection is untrusted.** The AC asked for stores deep-equal after backup, reset and restore, except `meta.updatedAt`. A backup is a file, and D6 says a file's scripts are untrusted until approved; a file that could say "trusted" about itself would be the supply-chain vector F41 names. Asked, the maintainer chose "mark untrusted". The AC as built: deep-equal except each collection's `scriptTrust`; `meta.updatedAt` is equal as well, since records are written back as they are. The AC text in section 9 carries the note. (2) The file is `wayfarer/workspace/2`: `{$id, exportedAt, stores}`, where `stores` holds `collections`, `folders`, `requests`, `environments`, `secrets` and `meta` as stored, and `history` when asked for. **The files of request bodies are not in it** ("full" in the task): a file may be 50 MB and an import is capped at 10 MB; a request names its file, as in a collection file, and says so when sent without it. Restore removes the stored body files. (3) Restore checks every record with the validators the single-store importers have (`validateCollection` per collection with its folders and requests, `validateEnvironmentExport`, `validateVaultFile`, `validateRows`, `validateRequestContent` for history), refuses the whole file with the reasons, and otherwise replaces the stores in one transaction and reloads the page. History is replaced only when the file has one. (4) `navigator.storage.persist()` is asked when a request or an environment is saved, and again at the next save when the browser said no. Settings shows the answer and `storage.estimate()`. (5) The reminder counts from the last backup, or from the first use when there is none, and is worked out when the page loads. "Not now" puts it off 14 days. (6) The Safari notice is shown in Settings when `navigator.vendor` is Apple's and the page is not installed (`display-mode: standalone`). (7) Environment export opens a dialog with the three choices. "With the vault" puts a vault file under the `vault` key of the environments file, and Secrets, "Import vault" reads it from there. "Plain text" needs the vault unlocked and `EXPORT SECRETS` typed. The Export button in Settings writes the default (nothing of protected variables). (8) **Claims:** C-046 (backup and restore) and C-047 (environment export) are new; 46. The limitation bullets for #73 and #74 are reworded to what remains true: a browser can still delete the data. Closes #73 (F16), #74 (F17). **Exit criteria, 2C: met.** Vault secrets reach the wire and are found nowhere in storage or exports (the scan test is green in 3 engines); the vault can be rotated, exported and locks itself; history is capped, searchable and redacted. **Budgets** reset in the P2.11 PR to the measurement after 2C (Appendix A). **Open at the end of 2C:** PR #181 (claim C-042), left alone as instructed; no finding is open from this session. |

The default is by content type (`html` in the media type, then `xml`); JSON over 5 MB opens as Text with a note. The URL is revoked by the effect's cleanup when the response or the view changes and when the viewer goes; Firefox still draws a revoked `blob:` image from its cache, so the e2e records the call to `URL.revokeObjectURL` instead of trying to load the URL again. (6) **Hex:** the first 64 KB, with the download for the rest; a body over the 50 MB display cap is a `Blob` and only its start is read. Text bodies have no hex view: the bytes are not kept once decoded. (9) **Headers tab:** rows are tracked by position, so a name sent twice is two rows (a browser joins repeats of one name itself; the bridge will not, P6.1). A response opened from history has no note: history does not keep the final URL unmasked. | 2026-10-10 | v1.2.5 | **Session 3A as executed**, one entry per PR. **Maintainer answers** (the prompt's three answers were left as placeholders; asked before any change): Q5, merge on green CI; D24 still holds; the two QuickJS packages are approved. **P3.0:** Phase 3 re-read against `main` at `c2bec7c` and split into 3A (P3.1, P3.8, P3.2, P3.7, P3.6) and 3B (P3.5, P3.3, P3.4, P3.9, P3.12, P3.10, P3.11, P3.13). The split is the one the session prompt suggested; the order inside each session is new, and the reasons are in section 9 under the delivery table. What the re-read corrected: (1) **F65** (#213): the executor drops a script's `logs` and `error`; P3.1 and P3.2 fix the halves. (2) C-006 changes in P3.1, which turns scripts on and deletes the banner; P3.11 keeps C-040 only. (3) The engine is in core and is handed the QuickJS module, so core's tests and the CLI run it in Node; the dependency is `quickjs-emscripten-core` plus the one variant, not the umbrella package that also depends on the debug and asyncify builds. The maintainer approved `quickjs-emscripten`; the core package is the part of it that is used. (4) The VM's global grows by task: `require` with P3.5, `postman` and the legacy globals with P3.4. (5) `scriptTrust.hash` arrives with P3.8, with no change of `DB_VERSION`: a missing hash is untrusted. (6) `pm.sendRequest` goes through `TransportRouter` (`RoutePolicy` is P6.4); `pm.cookies` is unsupported until P5.3; the runner-only calls have no effect until P5.2. (7) The redactor's path in section 4.2. (8) The release is task P3.13. (9) Two rules added from 2D's findings: a limit test asserts the message and not the time; the service worker is not changed to cache the `.wasm` file, since it already caches hashed same-origin files on first use (F64 stays as it is). Left for 3B to decide before it starts: whether P3.10's wall-clock gate can hold on a shared CI runner, and whether `newman` can enter the lockfile with `npm audit` at 0. No code changed. **P3.1:** as specified, with these decisions. (1) **Three files.** `packages/core/src/scripting/host.ts` (`runScript`: one runtime per run, the limits, the host functions), `vm-bootstrap.ts` beside it (what a script sees, as JavaScript text evaluated inside the VM before the script: `pm`, the small `expect`, `console`, `atob`, `btoa`, `setTimeout`), and the app's `quickjs.worker.ts`, which loads the engine once and passes messages. `ScriptSandbox` keeps its name and signature. (2) **The host object is never on the VM's global.** The bootstrap is a function called with it; a script reaches a host function only through the five names. Host functions take and return strings, numbers and booleans and check each argument's type, since a script can replace `String` under the bootstrap: a unit test does that and gets `Expected a string.`. So the rule P3.2 states is already how the boundary is built; P3.2 adds the caps, the console in the Tests tab and the redactor. (3) **The stack limit is 256 KB, not the plan's 1 MB, and it is not what stops a deep recursion in two engines of three.** Measured in a worker of the built app with a recursion 100,000 deep at limits from 128 KB to 4 MB and with none: QuickJS throws its own catchable "stack overflow" only when its count ends before the browser's stack under the WebAssembly does; that is at 128 KB in Chromium, at 256 KB in Firefox, and at no size tried in WebKit. Above that the browser throws from inside the engine call (`RangeError` in Chromium and WebKit, `InternalError: too much recursion` in Firefox). At 1 MB every engine did the second. `runScript` reports both as the stack limit, with one message, and after the second does not dispose the runtime (it would abort); `ScriptSandbox` ends the worker after any limit, so the next run has a new engine. A script may recurse about 1,500 calls deep in Firefox and between 740 and 1,500 in Chromium; `docs/scripts.md` says so. The AC of P3.6 (a recursion 100,000 deep gives a stack error) holds in all three. (4) **Timers.** A timer's callback stays in the VM; the host is told a number and a delay and asks for that number to be run. After the script, promise reactions and timers run until none is left or the deadline passes; a timer due after the deadline is not waited for. No `clearTimeout`, no `setInterval`: P3.1 names `setTimeout` only. (5) **The worker is kept between runs**, each run in a new runtime; a limit, a worker error or a failed load ends it. The page arms its own timer when the worker says the engine is loaded, one second past the script's deadline, so a cold download is not counted against the script. Part of P3.6 is therefore done here; P3.6 keeps the tests in three engines. (6) **Loading.** The worker imports the engine's `.wasm` file by its path under `node_modules`, and `".wasm": "file"` in `angular.json`'s `loader` gives `media/emscripten-module-<hash>.wasm` (503,134 B, 231,526 B gzip). The worker assembles the variant from the package's `./ffi` and `./emscripten-module` entry points with static imports: the worker is one lazy file of 26,796 B plus a 34,746 B chunk of `quickjs-emscripten-core`. The service worker is unchanged: its cache-first rule for hashed same-origin files takes the worker, the chunk and the `.wasm` file on first use, and `e2e/service-worker.spec.ts` runs a script with the server stopped. Initial bundle 1,209,737 B raw and 306,297 B gzip (1,209,797 and 306,352 before): budgets unchanged. (7) **CSP:** one keyword. C-011's test now asserts `script-src 'self' 'wasm-unsafe-eval'` exactly, in the header, the `<meta>` and on a script file. No violation event in any engine; nothing else was needed (R15 did not fire). (8) **F65, first half:** a script that ends in an error adds a failed row, "Pre-request script" or "Post-response script", after the tests that ran. The test was shown failing against the old behaviour. The request is still sent after a failed pre-request script, as before; Postman stops there, and P3.9 (3B) decides. (9) **`envMutations` is still a record**, built with `Object.fromEntries` from a `Map` and read with `Object.entries`; a variable named `__proto__` is an own key (unit test). An empty value still removes the variable. P3.3 replaces it with the scopes. (10) **Tests kept:** all ten of `script-sandbox.spec.ts` pass unchanged against QuickJS. The executor's "with scripts disabled never calls the sandbox" test had the deleted token as its subject; it is now "runs both scripts in every build" (C-006), and P3.8 brings back a skip for untrusted collections with its own test. The F01 tripwire asserts the script ran. (11) **Found in review, before merge: by the package's `./wasm` entry the import worked in the production build and failed under `ng serve`**, so scripts would have stopped running in local development. The development and unit-test servers hand an import that names a package to Vite, which tried to run the `.wasm` file as a WebAssembly module. Imported by path the file is built with the worker in all three. The e2e suite runs against the production build, so it could not show this; the check was one script test run against `ng serve`. **Two additions to `vitest.config.mts`, for the unit-test server only:** it puts the worker under the spec's directory, where the engine file is not (a middleware serves it from the package), and Vitest's browser mode rewrites `import()` to a helper that exists on the page and not in a worker (the worker files it serves get that helper; `quickjs-emscripten-core` imports its own module on demand). The built app needs neither. (12) **Licences:** the three packages are direct dependencies and are in `/3rdpartylicenses.txt` with QuickJS's own notice (Fabrice Bellard, MIT); nothing was added to `build-sw.mjs`. (13) C-006 reworded in the ledger, README and Trust Center; `docs/scripts.md` rewritten for QuickJS (P3.7 adds the test references). (14) **Found by CI on the PR:** the unit test of the memory limit filled 64 MB a megabyte at a time, which took over 5 s on the runner, so the script was stopped by its time limit first. The test now allocates 8 MB at a time with the deadline far away; the row is in `e2e/FLAKES.md`. Closes #58 (F01). **P3.8:** as specified in intent, with one change of form and these decisions. (1) **One digest per script, not one hash of the collection.** D6 and the task said "a SHA-256 hash of all scripts in the collection". With one hash, deleting or moving a request changes it, so either the user re-reviews a collection after deleting a request from it, or every write to the `requests` store re-hashes, which approves whatever was written, an importer's writes included. Neither is what D6 wants. With `approved: string[]`, a script is approved by its own text: removing a request needs nothing, and the only writes that add a digest are the review and a save from the composer. It is also stricter: a script changed by any other road has a digest the list does not hold. SHA-256 comes from WebCrypto, which is asynchronous, so digests are computed outside the IndexedDB transaction (one inside would end it) and written by `approveScripts` in a transaction of its own. If that write is lost the script stays unapproved: it fails closed. (2) **What is checked is the stored request the composer was loaded from, never the draft**: what the user types after loading is their own. The check runs when Send is pressed (`ScriptTrust.check`), from what is stored then; the banner reads a signal that follows it. A request with no stored script is still sent in the same task as the click. (3) **Saving does not launder.** The composer passes "were my scripts allowed to run" into Save and Save As; only then are the saved scripts' digests added, and only to a collection that is trusted. A script from an untrusted collection saved into a trusted one stays unapproved there and shows the banner; the unit test does exactly that. (4) **History.** A history entry carries the scripts of the request as sent, and opening one binds the composer to nothing, which would have counted as "written here". Its scripts wait for a review of their own (the same dialog, listing the entry's two scripts); the approval lasts while that entry is in the composer and stores nothing. An entry without scripts is not held. (5) **The review dialog** is one read-only Monaco editor holding every script under a comment line that names its request, not one editor per script: a collection of fifty requests would mount a hundred editors. (6) **The list only grows on edits** (64 characters per saved version of a script); a review starts it again. Marked in the code with its ceiling. (7) **Not done: collection- and folder-level scripts** do not exist until P4.9; when they arrive their digests join the same list. (8) The e2e seed helper writes the digests of the scripts it seeds, as a collection made here has them. (9) Screenshots of the banner, the Tests tab note and the dialog were read at 390 and 1440 px in both themes; the first showed the banner's icon alone on a row at 390 px, fixed. (10) Claim C-051; the limitation bullets for #98 are gone. The trust tests were shown failing with the check answering "yes" as before (5 of 8). Closes #98 (F41). **P3.2:** as specified, with these decisions. (1) **The boundary was built in P3.1** (strings, numbers and booleans, each argument's type checked by the host). P3.2 adds the AC's test: a script hands over an object with a getter, a prototype and a function, as a console argument, a variable's value and name, a test's name and a thrown value; the result is equal to its own JSON round trip and every value in it is a string. The getter is read once, inside the engine. (2) **Console caps:** 1,000 lines and 1 MB (characters); the line that passes the cap is cut to what fits, then one line `[console output truncated]`, and the script goes on. (3) **A cap the plan did not have:** test results and variables are kept by the host, outside the engine's 64 MB, so a loop of `pm.test` calls would have filled the worker's memory instead. They share 1 MB; the write that passes it throws into the script, and so does every write after it. (4) **The console is shown** in the Tests tab under "Console", the pre-request script's lines first (F65, second half). (5) **Masking.** Console lines, test names and messages and a script's own error go through `Redactor` with the request's vault secrets and credentials before they leave the executor: nothing unmasked reaches a signal. **A variable a post-response script sets is masked for vault secrets only.** Masking credentials there too would turn `pm.environment.set("token", pm.environment.get("token"))` into storing `***` over the user's own token, which is in the environment already; the invariant to keep is that a vault secret's plaintext is stored nowhere but the vault. A pre-request script's variables are stored as written: it runs before any secret is resolved and was given a secret only as its reference. P3.12 (3B) makes setting a protected variable write to the vault. (6) **The scan** (`e2e/secrets-wire.spec.ts`, C-007) gained a post-response script that reads the echoed secret and writes it to the console (twice), a variable, a test's name, a test's message and its own error; the scan reads the environments store and the Tests tab's text with everything else and finds 0 in three engines, and asserts the mask is there in its place. (7) Failure first: with the console dropped as before, `expected [] to deeply equal [ 'before', '[warn] careful', 'after' ]`. Closes #213 (F65). **P3.7:** as specified, with these decisions. (1) **Three tests in three engines** (`e2e/sandbox-escape.spec.ts`, claim C-052), each a script whose `pm.test` rows are the attempts; the page requires every row to pass and then looks from outside. (2) **Roads, not names:** 35 host names are looked up by eight roads to the global object (plain `typeof` through `Function`, indirect `eval`, `globalThis`, `Function('return this')()`, `({}).constructor.constructor`, a host function's constructor, an async function's and a generator's). The plan named four roads; a host function's constructor is the one that matters most, since a host function is the only object in the VM the host made. (3) **The global's names** are compared with the language's list plus the five, as in the unit test, and the one symbol QuickJS puts there itself (`Symbol.toStringTag`) is named. (4) **Network:** ten roads are called with a variable's value in the address and each must throw a `ReferenceError` or `TypeError`; a dynamic `import()` of an `https:` and of a `data:` address must be refused. The test listens on the browser context, which sees a worker's requests too, routes a canary host so that an escape would be counted and not lost in a DNS error, and asserts that the only request outside the app's origin is the user's own. `pm.sendRequest` does not exist yet, so there is no exception to make; P3.3 adds exactly the brokered calls. (5) **A forged result:** `postMessage`, `self` and `onmessage` do not exist, and no forged row appears. (6) **State:** a script that changes `Object.prototype`, `JSON`, `Array.prototype.map` and `String` runs three times and finds them whole each time; the page's own are untouched. (7) **Found by the suite:** `setTimeout` took a string and failed only when the timer fired ("not a function"), ending the script. Nothing was evaluated, but a refusal belongs at the call: it now throws a `TypeError` at once. Not given an F-number: no capability was reachable. (8) **The suite was shown to fail:** with `fetch` planted on the VM's global, nine rows fail (`found: fetch` by each road, `extra: fetch`). (9) `docs/scripts.md` describes the suite under the claim ID and the Trust Center's section is rewritten; the limitation bullet for #59 is gone. Closes #59 (F02). **P3.6:** as specified, with one finding. (1) **F66 (#220): the memory limit did not hold.** The first run of this task's own e2e test, a pre-request script that takes 25 typed arrays of 8 MB, ended without an error in three engines. Cause: QuickJS built for WebAssembly counts 8 bytes for an allocation whatever its size (its `malloc_usable_size` returns 0 under Emscripten), so `setMemoryLimit` refuses a single allocation above the limit and nothing else. Measured in Node under the 64 MB limit: 1,840 MB of typed arrays held before the first error, the engine's memory at its 2 GB maximum. The unit test of P3.1 had not seen it because it asked for one allocation of 200 MB, and for an endless loop that stopped at 2 GB with the same message. This contradicts the plan's design (`setMemoryLimit(64 MB)` as the limit) and the docs P3.1 wrote; it is not an escape, and the design closes it, so the session went on and says so in its report. **Fix:** the engine is loaded with a `WebAssembly.Memory` whose maximum is the limit (`wasmMemory` in `newVariant`; `scriptMemory()` in core gives the size, the worker makes the object). Growth past it fails in the browser, the engine's allocator returns nothing, QuickJS throws "out of memory". Measured with the cap: typed arrays, megabyte strings, arrays of numbers and small objects without end each stop within 150 ms with the memory at 61 MB, and the engine runs the next script. With no memory left QuickJS cannot make its error object and throws `null`; `runScript` reports that as the memory limit too. The limit is now on the whole engine, its own few hundred kilobytes included. Four unit tests (`F66: stops a script that allocates 200 MB in pieces…`) were shown failing against the uncapped engine, and the e2e against the built app. `setMemoryLimit` is kept: it refuses a single huge allocation before the memory is asked to grow. (2) **The three limits in the escape suite** (`limits`, three tests in three engines): the pre-request script runs into the limit, the Tests tab shows the message, the script's `catch` did not run, and the post-response script of the same send passes, twice over. The engine is fetched at least three times in the test, which is the worker being ended and started. Messages: "Script timed out after 5000 ms", "Script exceeded memory limit (64 MB)", "Script exceeded the stack limit (too much recursion)". No test asserts a duration; the test's own timeout is 120 s. (3) **The page's own timer** (one second past the deadline, armed when the worker says the engine is loaded) and the recycling were built in P3.1; P3.6 adds their unit tests with a stand-in worker: a run that is never answered ends at deadline plus one second with the timeout message and ends the worker; a result with a limit ends the worker and every run still waiting on it; a message that names no run is ignored. The ten tests of `script-sandbox.spec.ts` are unchanged. (4) Session 3A's numbers are in Appendix A. Budgets are unchanged: the initial bundle moved by 7,885 B raw (0.65%) over the session and is within the limits set in P2.19. Closes #220 (F66). |
| 2026-10-10 | v1.2.6 | **Session 3B as executed**, one entry per PR. **Maintainer answers** (the prompt's six answers were left as placeholders; asked before any change): Q5, merge on green CI, security, claims and release PRs included; D24 still holds; a failed pre-request script stops the send, as in Postman; P3.10's gate: measure first and propose; the new dependencies are approved (`@noble/hashes`, `chai`, `crypto-js` 4.2.0, `lodash`, `uuid`, `moment`; `newman` as a dev dependency); v2.1.0 is tagged when P3.13's CI is green. **P3.5:** as specified, with these decisions. (1) **Built by `npm ci`, not committed.** `scripts/build-vm-libs.mjs` (esbuild, now a named dev dependency; it was in the tree through `@angular/build`) writes each library as one module whose default export is its text, into `packages/core/src/scripting/vm-libs/`, with `licenses.txt` beside them; the root package's `prepare` script runs it, so lint, the type check, the tests and the build find the files after `npm ci` with no step of their own. The folder is in `.gitignore`. (2) **A library is text, evaluated inside the VM** by the engine's own `eval` (kept by the bootstrap before a script can replace the name). The host hands the text over as a string: no handle crosses, and a library has the script's global and nothing more. The escape suite looks the 35 host names up again with all five loaded, also through Lodash's `get` and `template`. (3) **`require` cannot wait for a download** (the engine is the sync variant, section 5), so the libraries a script names are fetched before it starts: `librariesOf` finds a library by its name written in a script that says `require`. A name put together at run time is refused with a message that says to write it out. Marked in the code with its ceiling. What a script can require does not depend on which scripts ran before: `runScript` is given the libraries of this script only. The worker fetches them before it says "started", so a download is not counted against the script, as with the engine. (4) **The loader is a module of its own**, `@wayfarer/core/library-loader` (a second `paths` entry). Exported from the package's index, every library was emitted twice: the bundler makes a file of each `import()` it meets, in the page's graph too, though nothing there calls it. (5) **What a library looks for in a browser is an argument of its wrapper**, never a global: `self` and `crypto` hold `getRandomValues` backed by the host (crypto-js salts, `uuid.v4`), and `EventTarget` and `Event` are stand-ins that do nothing (chai 6 announces plugins through them). (6) **Versions:** chai 6.3.0 (it bundles its own former dependencies and ships one licence), crypto-js 4.2.0 pinned exactly (R18), lodash 4.18.1, moment 2.31.0 from its `min/moment.min.js`, which has no locales (the main file loads a locale by a computed path, which the bundler turns into every locale), uuid 14.0.3, `@noble/hashes` 2.4.0. All MIT. `npm audit`: 0. (7) **Host-native crypto:** `MD5`, `SHA1`, `SHA256`, `HmacSHA1`, `HmacSHA256` and `enc.Utf8/Hex/Base64`, as the task lists; a WordArray crosses as JSON text (`[sigBytes, word, ...]`), which the engine parses and writes with native code. The host checks the JSON is a list of numbers. The incremental `algo.*` API and everything else stay crypto-js's own code; the unit test uses that as the oracle for the hashes (45 texts, as text and as a WordArray), and Node's WebCrypto for the HMACs (100 random key and message pairs, both hashes). (8) **Memory (F66):** all five loaded stay inside the engine's first 16 MB; a script then held 56 MB of text before the limit, against 58 MB with none loaded. Loading all five takes about 75 ms in Node. (9) **Sizes**, each a lazy file: lodash 73,413 B (26,255 B gzip), crypto-js 70,529 B (25,827), chai 66,226 B (17,465), moment 60,949 B (19,691), uuid 10,772 B (4,128). The script worker is 42,635 B (27,731 after 3A: the host's hashes and the wider bootstrap). The initial bundle is 1,217,682 B raw, the same byte count as after 3A; budgets unchanged. The service worker is unchanged: the files carry a content hash, and the offline test now requires a library. No CSP change, 0 violation events in three engines (R15 did not fire). (10) **Licences:** the build script reads the packages that went into each library from the bundler's own list and writes their licence files; `build-sw.mjs` appends them to `/3rdpartylicenses.txt`. `@noble/hashes` is bundled by Angular into the worker and is listed by `extractLicenses`. Nothing else was touched; the claim's sentence says `require` gives five libraries and no other module (C-052, README, Trust Center, ledger). (12) **Found on the way, in code from 3A:** with the larger bootstrap, the F66 test "small objects without end" ended with "Script failed." instead of the memory limit. (1) **The matrix is data, and the suite runs it twice.** `packages/core/test/pm-compat/cases.ts` holds one row per part of Postman's API (102 rows: 66 supported, 20 partial with a note, 16 unsupported), each with a script that proves what the row says; `pm-compat.test.ts` runs them in Node and `src/app/shared/scripts/pm-compat.spec.ts` runs the same rows through `ScriptSandbox` in the real worker in a browser. A change with nowhere to go (no active environment, a request in no collection) is a line in the console. `replaceIn` is core's `VariableResolver` over the scopes as the script has left them, on every one of the objects (a partial row says so). (3) **`pm.request`.** A script is given the request as composed (`scriptRequestOf`: the rows that are switched on; of a multipart form or a file only the mode). A pre-request script's changes come back as JSON text, which the host checks field by field, and `withScriptRequest` puts them into the request that is built; history keeps the request as the user composed it. So the first half of P3.9 (changes apply before resolution) is done here; P3.9 keeps the resolved request for the post-response script, the three durations and the failed pre-request script. D6 already says so (a script plus secret access is an exfiltration path; the control is the review), and P3.9 gives a post-response script the resolved request. While a request is in flight and the script has nothing else to run, the engine's deadline moves by the wait, and the page's watchdog is off until the answer is back. It covers set, get and clear of environment and global variables across requests, `tests[...]` with true, false, a text and 0, `responseBody`, `responseCode`, `responseTime`, `responseHeaders`, `postman.getResponseHeader` in any case, the `request` object, `iteration`, `setNextRequest`, a 404, and a variable in the address set by a pre-request script. (4) **Ten more names on the VM's global** (`postman`, `tests`, `responseBody`, `responseCode`, `responseTime`, `responseHeaders`, `request`, `environment`, `globals`, `iteration`), in the allow-lists of `host.spec.ts` and the escape suite; C-052's sentences say so. `postman.*` are the functions of `pm`, so there is one implementation. `tests` is read when the script has ended; a falsy value fails with Newman's own message ("expected false to be truthy"). (5) Beyond the plan's list, since each is one line: `getGlobalVariable`, `clearGlobalVariable`, `clearEnvironmentVariables`, `clearGlobalVariables`. Not there: the library globals of the old sandbox (`_`, `CryptoJS`, `tv4`, `cheerio`, `xml2Json`), one unsupported row. (6) The matrix has 21 more rows (123: 80 supported, 26 partial, 17 unsupported), run in Node and in the worker like the rest. (7) Found while writing the script: a synchronous `execFileSync` blocks the echo server that lives in the same process, and Newman then waits for ever; the script uses the asynchronous call. (8) Failure first: `'responseBody' is not defined`. (9) **In the same PR, a second commit: the claims projects wait 15 s for an expectation on GitHub's runners** (`CLAIMS_EXPECT_MS`), 5 s elsewhere. `@claim:C-003` failed once on PR #224's WebKit job and `@claim:C-046` once on PR #226's, each green on the rerun of the same commit: the fourth and fifth slow WebKit jobs since 2D, and `e2e/FLAKES.md` had said after the third what a fourth would get. No assertion changed and none asserts a duration; a claim test still has no retry. It is in this PR and not one of its own because both touch the register, and each CI round on a PR costs a quarter of an hour. **P3.9:** as specified, with one decision of the maintainer's and four of the session's. **Q6 was asked before the first change and answered yes; asked again when the session found its own description of the check had been wrong** (it had said the comparison "would miss nothing"; it misses a host that a script stored in a variable in an earlier send), **the maintainer chose the one-send prompt with a narrow claim.** As built: (1) **A failed pre-request script stops the send** (maintainer, above). `RequestExecutor.execute` throws `PreRequestScriptError` (a `SendBlockedError`) before the request is built: no vault read, no request, no history entry. It carries the rows of the tests that ran, the failed row, the console and the script's duration; `WorkspaceStore` shows them and opens the Tests tab, and the response panel shows for test rows alone. What the script set before the error is stored, as in Postman. "Failed" is an error (a throw, a syntax error, a limit); a `pm.test` that fails does not stop anything. (2) **The post-response script sees the request as sent, every vault secret masked.** `sentScriptRequest` (core) builds the view from what was built: method, address, headers with the Auth tab's among them, a text body as sent, form fields read back from the sent text, and of a multipart form or a file the mode as before. **The session's decision, not the plan's:** the plan says "the resolved request" and no more; handing a script the plaintext of a vault secret would have been the first place the app does that (a variable's value is the reference, `replaceIn` leaves it, `pm.sendRequest` refuses it), and with `pm.sendRequest` it would have been one line to send it on. The masking is the `Redactor` over the request's secrets, applied to the wire text before the view is made; a credential that is not a vault secret is shown, since a script can read it from the environment anyway. The matrix row `pm.request (post-response)` is therefore "partial", and the legacy `request` row's note says the same (123 rows: 79 supported, 27 partial, 17 unsupported). The maintainer can reverse this in one line of `execute`. What it does not do: a server that sends a secret back hands it to the script in the response, as before. (3) **Three durations.** `RequestExecutionResult.timings` holds `preScriptMs`, `requestMs` and `postScriptMs`; the Tests tab shows them on one line when a script ran ("Pre-request script 12 ms · Request 340 ms · Post-response script 8 ms"). A script's time is the wall time of `ScriptSandbox.execute`, so the first script of a session counts the engine's download and a script counts its `pm.sendRequest` calls; the docs say so. The request's time was already the transport call alone (F11) and is unchanged. (4) **Q6, claim C-053.** `WorkspaceStore.resolveRequest` resolves the host (name and port) of the address as composed with the variables as they were before the pre-request script, and of the address as the script left it with the variables as they are; when they differ and the request refers to a vault secret, `Confirm` asks, naming both, before the vault is read. A no throws `SendDeclinedError`; the executor then sets every variable the script touched back to the value it had (or removes it), in all three scopes. **Without that undo the prompt was a formality:** the script's variables are stored before the request is built, so after "Don't send" a second Send would have found the changed host already in the environment, seen no difference, and sent the secret there unasked. A unit test sends twice and is asked twice. Known edge: a disabled row of the same name that the script's `set` replaced is not brought back. **What the claim does not cover, and says so in the README, the Trust Center and `docs/scripts.md`:** a host that a post-response script, or the pre-request script of a request without a secret, stored in a variable is by the next send the variable's value, and nothing asks. Closing that needs the app to remember which variables a script wrote (a stored shape), which no task has; it is noted for Phase 4 in section 0. A path, a query or a header changed on the same host is not asked about. (5) **Tests changed because the plan changes what they describe:** the unit test of F65 had both scripts fail and asserted "the request is still sent": the failing script there is now the post-response one, and a new test holds the pre-request case to the opposite. The e2e test of F65 swapped its two scripts for the same reason. **The three `@claim:C-052` limit tests** met their limit in the pre-request script and proved "the next script runs" with the post-response script of the same send; that send no longer happens. The limit is now met by the post-response script and the next script is the following send's pre-request script, over three sends instead of two, so that the engine is still loaded three times and the assertion on that count is unchanged; the request count is 3 for 2. The P3.3 unit test that said the post-response script is given the script-changed template is given the built request. No assertion was removed. (6) New tests: 1 in core, 6 in the executor's spec, 8 in `composer-scripts.spec.ts` (two of them `@claim:C-053`), 2 e2e for the ACs in `scripts.spec.ts` (the header reaches `/echo`; the script's 200 ms is in its number and the request's number is smaller than the script's, a relation and not a bound, R6), 1 e2e claim test in `script-redirect.spec.ts`. The 200 ms itself is asserted exactly in the unit test, with the clock stubbed. `protectVariable` moved to `e2e/support/app.ts` for the two specs that share it. (7) Failure first: `PreRequestScriptError` and `timings` did not exist (compile errors), then 5 of 8 composer tests. **P3.12:** as specified. (1) `RequestExecutor.applyChanges` is the one place a script's variable changes are stored; it now hands each scope's changes to `intoVault` first. A `set` on a variable whose stored value is one `{{$secret.<id>}}` reference and nothing else (`protectedSecretId`) is encrypted into that secret with the new `SecretsVault.replaceSecret` (the ciphertext is made before the transaction; `SecretsRepository.replaceCipher` replaces the envelope of a secret that is there and keeps its id and name) and is left out of the changes handed to `changeEnvironment`, `changeGlobals` and `changeCollectionVariables` (P2.10). The variable keeps its reference. (2) **Locked vault:** refused, with `[warn] pm.environment.set("token"): this variable is protected and the vault is locked, so its value was not changed.` The user is not asked for the passphrase in the middle of a script. The request that follows a pre-request script still asks for it when it uses a secret, as before. (3) **Beyond the task's words:** the same in `pm.globals` and `pm.collectionVariables` (a reference can be typed into either); a script that writes back the reference it read changes nothing (without this, a script that copies its variables would have encrypted the text of the reference over the secret); a reference to a secret the vault no longer has is refused with a line of its own; `unset` removes the variable and leaves the secret. (4) **A post-response script's value for a protected variable is not masked** on its way into the vault: the vault is where a secret belongs. Every other value is masked as before. (5) **Known and left:** "protected" is read from this tab's copy of the rows, so a variable another tab protected in the same moment is written as text (marked in the code with what would close it); and when the user answers "Don't send" to C-053's question, a secret the script had already replaced stays replaced, since the old plaintext is gone. (6) Tests: `script-secrets.integration.spec.ts` runs the executor over the real IndexedDB and the real vault: unlocked from a pre-request and from a post-response script, locked, the reference written back, a deleted secret, `unset`; it reads every record of all eight stores and finds neither the new nor the old plaintext. (1) **The benchmark** is `packages/core/bench/script.bench.ts`, run by `npm -w packages/core run bench` (a Vitest config of its own, so it is not part of `npm test` and not in the coverage). It is the task's script: an HMAC-SHA256 over a 1 KB text with crypto-js, the JSON of a 1 MB response, 50 `pm.test` assertions with chai. Each run is a whole `runScript`: a new runtime, the bootstrap, and both libraries evaluated again, which is what a send pays. It asserts that the 50 tests ran and passed, so a script that did no work cannot record a good time. (2) **Numbers:** Appendix A. On a laptop about 38 ms in Node and 36 to 43 ms in the three browsers; on GitHub's runner 64 to 88 ms. R3's limit is 300 ms: the risk did not come true, because the hashes and the response's JSON are done by native code (P3.5, and QuickJS's own `JSON.parse`). (3) **The 25% rule cannot hold, measured:** five jobs of the same commit gave p95 64.3 to 88.4 ms, a spread of 37%. A second measure was tried and dropped: the script's time divided by the time the host's own `JSON.parse` takes for the same body in the same process, which should cancel a slow machine; it ran from 18 to 24 over the same five jobs, 33%, no steadier than the time itself. So was a count of the engine's interrupt callbacks, which is the same on every machine but comes to 8 for this script: too coarse, and blind to the native parts R3 is about. (4) **The gate (maintainer, of three options: this, a 200 ms ceiling, or report only):** the Core job fails when p95 is over 300 ms or the engine's load over 250 ms, which is 3.4 times the slowest reading. It catches a regression of the kind R3 names and not drift; drift is seen in Appendix A, where each session's row records the numbers again. **The AC's "CI fails if p95 regresses more than 25%" is replaced by this**, and the Phase 3 exit criterion "the benchmark gate passes" means this gate. (5) **Where it runs:** in Node, in the Core job, not on "the Chromium runner" of the task's text: V8 runs the same WebAssembly in both, the Core job has no browser to start, and the three browsers measured by hand agree with Node within a few milliseconds. The plan's "WASM cold load" in a browser includes a download, which a CI gate would measure as the runner's network; the one-off readings are in Appendix A. (6) The measurement was taken in the draft of this PR, with a CI step that only printed; the gate was added after the answer. **P3.11:** as specified. (1) `scripts/gen-compat-doc.mjs` (`npm run gen:compat`) writes `docs/postman-compatibility.md` from `CASES`: 123 rows (79 supported, 27 partial, 17 not supported) in 19 sections, one per object of `pm`, one for the older sandbox, one for `console`, timers and encoding, one for `require`. It imports `cases.ts` as it is: Node 24 runs a TypeScript file that needs no compiler, and that file holds data and one interface. **"Generated from the conformance suite" means from its rows, not from a run:** a row is in the document because it is in the file both test runs iterate (`pm-compat.test.ts` in Node, `pm-compat.spec.ts` in the worker), and a row that does not hold fails those. (2) **CI fails on a diff twice over:** `npm run check:compat` in the lint job beside `check:csp`, and a test in `test:scripts` that compares the committed file with what the rows give. (3) **Anchors.** The error messages have linked to `docs/postman-compatibility.md#pm-cookies` and seven other sections since P3.3 and P3.5, and no such file existed. GitHub's anchor for a heading "`pm.cookies`" would be `pmcookies`, so each section carries an explicit `<a id>`: `pm-` and the object's name in lower case, `require`, `legacy`, `globals`. A test reads the anchors out of `vm-bootstrap.ts` (the `unsupported(…, "anchor")` calls and the names given to `refusing`) and every written-out link in the engine and the executor, requires the eight known today to be among them, and requires a section for each. (4) **C-040 reworded**, with its ledger row, the README bullet and its tests: from "a small `pm.*` subset" to "the parts of Postman's script API that `docs/postman-compatibility.md` lists", the document being written from the matrix. The marker is in the README and in the generated document. Its tests are the two it had (Monaco loads; a script runs in the sandbox), every row of the matrix in Node and in the worker (the `it.each` titles carry the tag), and the two tests of the document. `scripts/check-claims.mjs` could not read the title of `it.each(table)("title")`; it can now, with a test. (1) **A script's value never replaces a stored reference.** `VariableChange` has an optional `keepSecret`; `applyVariableChanges` leaves out a set so marked when a row of that name holds one vault secret's reference, and it runs inside the store's own transaction on the stored rows. `RequestExecutor.applyChanges` marks every change a script makes. So the tab's copy of the rows only decides whether a value is encrypted into the vault; when that copy is a moment old the value is dropped, not stored as text. The editor's changes are not marked: unprotecting a variable is saving its text. Six expectations in `request-executor.spec.ts` name the mark on the changes they assert. (2) **"Don't send" puts a replaced secret back.** `intoVault` reads the secret's plaintext before it replaces it and keeps it, in memory, for that send; the undo of C-053's question writes it back after the variables. Failure first, both in `script-secrets.integration.spec.ts`: "expected 'new-plaintext-7b2e4d' to be 'old-plaintext-3f9a1c'", and the script's text found where the reference had been. Twelve fixtures plus `HEAD`, in three engines. (7) **What is run and what is not:** the `fetch` code is run by Node and the cURL command by bash, against the echo server, in core's tests and in the e2e; Python and HTTPie are held to snapshots only, since neither is installed where the tests run. An address that starts with `-` goes to curl as `--url`. HTTPie reads method, address and items by position: one that starts with `-` writes no command. Each has a test. **Sizes:** the generators are a lazy file of their own (Appendix A). |

## Appendix B — Audit evidence (2026-09-27, production)

- Prod sandbox worker `worker-SEEDX3S5.js` served with `content-security-policy: … script-src 'self' …`. Posting a run message returned `error: "Evaluating a string as JavaScript violates the following Content Security Policy directive because 'unsafe-eval' is not an allowed source of script"`.
- `GET https://httpbin.org/html` rendered `200 OK` with body `{"error": {}, "text": "<!DOCTYPE html>…"}`. `/image/png` rendered binary as text.
- A request to an unresolvable host rendered `504 Gateway Timeout` in about 20 ms.
- Page console on load: Cloudflare Insights beacon blocked by CSP; an inline Cloudflare challenge script blocked by CSP.
- The accessibility snapshot showed buttons named `bolt`, `upload` and `light_mode`.
